task-0069-integer-partitions · Integer partitions

Prompt 
Write `partitions(n)` in Python returning the number of integer partitions of `n` — the count of ways to write `n` as a sum of positive integers where order does not matter. n is in [1, 60]. Use the coin-change style DP over the coin values 1..n.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def partitions(n):
    dp = [0] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 1
    for coin in range(1, n + 1):
        for j in range(coin, n + 1):
            dp[j] += dp[j - coin]
    return dp[n]

task-0038-ambiguous-trick-wording · Ambiguous trick wording

Prompt 
A bat and a ball cost $1.10 in total. The bat costs $1.00 more than the ball. How much does the ball cost?
Model answer 
**$0.05 (5 cents)**

Let the ball cost = x
The bat costs x + $1.00

Total: x + (x + $1.00) = $1.10
2x + $1.00 = $1.10
2x = $0.10
x = $0.05

Check: Ball = $0.05, Bat = $1.05, Total = $1.10 ✓ (and the bat is exactly $1.00 more than the ball)

task-0249-longest-valid-parentheses · Longest valid parentheses

Prompt 
Write `longestValidParentheses(s)` returning the length of the longest valid (well-formed) parentheses substring. Characters are '(' and ')'. Stack or two-pass scan.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function longestValidParentheses(s) {
  let stack = [-1];
  let max = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) {
    if (s[i] === '(') {
      stack.push(i);
    } else {
      stack.pop();
      if (stack.length === 0) {
        stack.push(i);
      } else {
        max = Math.max(max, i - stack[stack.length - 1]);
      }
    }
  }
  return max;
}
```

task-0090-can-place-flowers · Can place flowers

Prompt 
Write `canPlaceFlowers(flowerbed, n)` where flowerbed[i] is 0 (empty) or 1 (flower). Return true if you can plant n new flowers without planting adjacent to existing or new ones.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `canPlaceFlowers`.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function canPlaceFlowers(flowerbed, n) {
  let count = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < flowerbed.length; i++) {
    if (
      flowerbed[i] === 0 &&
      (i === 0 || flowerbed[i - 1] === 0) &&
      (i === flowerbed.length - 1 || flowerbed[i + 1] === 0)
    ) {
      flowerbed[i] = 1;
      count++;
      if (count >= n) return true;
    }
  }
  return count >= n;
}
```

task-0046-monty-hall-with-variant · Monty Hall with variant

Prompt 
Four doors hide one car and three goats. You pick a door. The host, who knows the location, intentionally opens two of the other doors, revealing goats, and offers you the chance to switch to the one remaining unopened door. What is the probability of winning the car if you switch?
Model answer 
**3/4 (75%)**

**Reasoning:**

With 4 doors, your initial pick has a **1/4** chance of being the car and a **3/4** chance of being a goat.

- **If your pick is the car (1/4):** The host opens two goat doors; the remaining door hides a goat. Switching → **lose**.
- **If your pick is a goat (3/4):** Among the other three doors, one hides the car and two hide goats. The host (who knows the location) must open the two goat doors, leaving the car as the single remaining unopened door. Switching → **win**.

So switching wins exactly when your initial pick was a goat:

$$P(\text{win by switching}) = \frac{3}{4}$$

(Staying wins only if your first pick was correct: 1/4.)

task-0235-maximum-product-subarray · Maximum product subarray

Prompt 
Write `maxProduct(nums)` returning the maximum product of any contiguous (non-empty) subarray. `nums` can contain negatives and zeros. O(n) with tracking max/min.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function maxProduct(nums) {
  if (!nums || nums.length === 0) return 0;

  let maxProd = nums[0];
  let minProd = nums[0];
  let result = nums[0];

  for (let i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
    const num = nums[i];
    if (num < 0) {
      const temp = maxProd;
      maxProd = minProd;
      minProd = temp;
    }
    maxProd = Math.max(num, maxProd * num);
    minProd = Math.min(num, minProd * num);
    result = Math.max(result, maxProd);
  }

  return result;
}

task-0078-integer-to-roman · Integer to Roman

Prompt 
Write `intToRoman(num)` converting an integer 1..3999 to a Roman numeral. Use standard symbols and subtractive forms (IV, IX, XL, XC, CD, CM).

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `intToRoman`.
Model answer 
export function intToRoman(num) {
  const pairs = [
    [1000, 'M'], [900, 'CM'], [500, 'D'], [400, 'CD'],
    [100, 'C'], [90, 'XC'], [50, 'L'], [40, 'XL'],
    [10, 'X'], [9, 'IX'], [5, 'V'], [4, 'IV'], [1, 'I']
  ];
  let result = '';
  for (const [value, symbol] of pairs) {
    while (num >= value) {
      result += symbol;
      num -= value;
    }
  }
  return result;
}

task-0240-trapping-rain-water · Trapping rain water

Prompt 
Write `trap(height)` in Python returning how much water can be trapped between the bars described by `height`. O(n) two-pointer or left/right max arrays.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def trap(height):
    if not height or len(height) < 3:
        return 0
    left, right = 0, len(height) - 1
    left_max, right_max = height[left], height[right]
    water = 0
    while left < right:
        if left_max < right_max:
            left += 1
            left_max = max(left_max, height[left])
            water += left_max - height[left]
        else:
            right -= 1
            right_max = max(right_max, height[right])
            water += right_max - height[right]
    return water

task-0242-minimum-path-in-grid · Minimum path in grid

Prompt 
Write `minPathSum(grid)` returning the minimum sum from top-left to bottom-right moving only down or right. `grid` is m x n of non-negative numbers. DP.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function minPathSum(grid) {
  if (!grid || grid.length === 0 || grid[0].length === 0) return 0;
  const m = grid.length;
  const n = grid[0].length;
  const dp = Array.from({ length: m }, () => new Array(n).fill(0));
  dp[0][0] = grid[0][0];
  for (let i = 1; i < m; i++) dp[i][0] = dp[i - 1][0] + grid[i][0];
  for (let j = 1; j < n; j++) dp[0][j] = dp[0][j - 1] + grid[0][j];
  for (let i = 1; i < m; i++) {
    for (let j = 1; j < n; j++) {
      dp[i][j] = Math.min(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]) + grid[i][j];
    }
  }
  return dp[m - 1][n - 1];
}

task-0282-lru-cache-operator · LRU cache operator

Prompt 
A cache agent manages an LRU (least-recently-used) cache of `capacity` slots. It processes a list of key `accesses` (integers). A 'hit' occurs when the key is already in the cache (it is moved to most-recent); a 'miss' occurs otherwise. On a miss the key is inserted, and if the cache is full the least-recently-used key is evicted. Write `lruAccess(capacity, accesses)` returning the total number of cache misses. If capacity is 0, every access is a miss.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function lruAccess(capacity, accesses) {
  if (capacity <= 0) {
    return accesses.length;
  }
  const cache = new Map();
  let misses = 0;
  for (const key of accesses) {
    if (cache.has(key)) {
      cache.delete(key);
      cache.set(key, true);
    } else {
      misses++;
      if (cache.size >= capacity) {
        const lruKey = cache.keys().next().value;
        cache.delete(lruKey);
      }
      cache.set(key, true);
    }
  }
  return misses;
}

task-0107-number-of-good-pairs · Number of good pairs

Prompt 
Write `numIdenticalPairs(nums)` returning the number of pairs (i, j) with i < j and nums[i] === nums[j].

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `numIdenticalPairs`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function numIdenticalPairs(nums) {
  const counts = new Map();
  let result = 0;
  for (const num of nums) {
    const prev = counts.get(num) || 0;
    result += prev;
    counts.set(num, prev + 1);
  }
  return result;
}

task-0236-longest-increasing-subsequence · Longest increasing subsequence

Prompt 
Write `lis_length(nums)` in Python returning the length of the longest strictly increasing subsequence. O(n log n) using patience sorting.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
```python
import bisect


def lis_length(nums):
    tails = []
    for x in nums:
        i = bisect.bisect_left(tails, x)
        if i == len(tails):
            tails.append(x)
        else:
            tails[i] = x
    return len(tails)
```

task-0231-course-schedule-cycle-check · Course schedule cycle check

Prompt 
Write `canFinish(numCourses, prerequisites)` returning true if all courses can be finished. `prerequisites[i] = [a, b]` means you must take course b before course a. Return false if there is a cycle. Topological sort / Kahn's algorithm.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function canFinish(numCourses, prerequisites) {
  const inDegree = new Array(numCourses).fill(0);
  const adj = Array.from({ length: numCourses }, () => []);

  for (const [a, b] of prerequisites) {
    adj[b].push(a);
    inDegree[a]++;
  }

  const queue = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < numCourses; i++) {
    if (inDegree[i] === 0) queue.push(i);
  }

  let visited = 0;
  while (queue.length > 0) {
    const node = queue.shift();
    visited++;
    for (const next of adj[node]) {
      inDegree[next]--;
      if (inDegree[next] === 0) queue.push(next);
    }
  }

  return visited === numCourses;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0136-median-of-two-sorted-arrays · Median of two sorted arrays

Prompt 
Write `findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2)` returning the median of the two sorted arrays combined. If the combined length is even, return the average of the two middle numbers (e.g. 2.5). O(log(m+n)).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `findMedianSortedArrays`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2) {
  if (nums1.length > nums2.length) {
    const tmp = nums1;
    nums1 = nums2;
    nums2 = tmp;
  }

  const m = nums1.length;
  const n = nums2.length;

  let low = 0;
  let high = m;

  while (low <= high) {
    const partition1 = Math.floor((low + high) / 2);
    const partition2 = Math.floor((m + n + 1) / 2) - partition1;

    const maxLeft1 = partition1 === 0 ? -Infinity : nums1[partition1 - 1];
    const minRight1 = partition1 === m ? Infinity : nums1[partition1];

    const maxLeft2 = partition2 === 0 ? -Infinity : nums2[partition2 - 1];
    const minRight2 = partition2 === n ? Infinity : nums2[partition2];

    if (maxLeft1 <= minRight2 && maxLeft2 <= minRight1) {
      if ((m + n) % 2 === 0) {
        return (Math.max(maxLeft1, maxLeft2) + Math.min(minRight1, minRight2)) / 2;
      }
      return Math.max(maxLeft1, maxLeft2);
    } else if (maxLeft1 > minRight2) {
      high = partition1 - 1;
    } else {
      low = partition1 + 1;
    }
  }

  throw new Error("Input arrays are not sorted");
}

task-0087-counting-bits · Counting bits

Prompt 
Write `countBits(n)` returning an array ans of length n+1 where ans[i] is the number of 1-bits in the binary representation of i, for all 0..n. O(n) time.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `countBits`.
Model answer 
export function countBits(n) {
  const ans = new Array(n + 1);
  ans[0] = 0;
  for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
    ans[i] = ans[i >> 1] + (i & 1);
  }
  return ans;
}

Each bit is ANDed: only bit 3 (value 8) is set in both numbers.

task-0273-keyword-ranking-agent · Keyword ranking agent

Prompt 
A content-analysis agent ranks keywords. Given `text` and an integer `k`, write `topWords(text, k)` returning the top `k` most frequent words (sequences of letters only, case-insensitive). Tally frequencies; sort by descending frequency, and break ties by ascending word. Return the top `k` words. If fewer than `k` distinct words exist, return all of them.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function topWords(text, k) {
  const counts = new Map();
  const matches = text.match(/[a-zA-Z]+/g) || [];
  for (const w of matches) {
    const lower = w.toLowerCase();
    counts.set(lower, (counts.get(lower) || 0) + 1);
  }
  const words = [...counts.keys()];
  words.sort((a, b) => {
    if (counts.get(b) !== counts.get(a)) return counts.get(b) - counts.get(a);
    if (a < b) return -1;
    if (a > b) return 1;
    return 0;
  });
  return words.slice(0, k);
}

task-0166-merge-intervals · Merge intervals

Prompt 
Given intervals as [start, end] pairs (end inclusive), merge all overlapping intervals and return merged intervals (sorted by start). Example: [[1,3],[2,6],[8,10],[15,18]] -> [[1,6],[8,10],[15,18]].

```js
// BUGGY
export function mergeIntervals(intervals) {
  intervals.sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);
  const out = [];
  for (const [s, e] of intervals) {
    if (out.length && s <= out[out.length - 1][1]) {
      out[out.length - 1] = [out[out.length - 1][0], e];
    } else {
      out.push([s, e]);
    }
  }
  return out;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `mergeIntervals`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
```js
export function mergeIntervals(intervals) {
  intervals.sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);
  const out = [];
  for (const [s, e] of intervals) {
    if (out.length && s <= out[out.length - 1][1]) {
      out[out.length - 1][1] = Math.max(out[out.length - 1][1], e);
    } else {
      out.push([s, e]);
    }
  }
  return out;
}
```

task-0196-trapping-rain-water · Trapping rain water

Prompt 
Given n non-negative integers representing an elevation map (bar widths of 1), compute how much water it can trap after raining. Example: [0,1,0,2,1,0,1,3,2,1,2,1] -> 6.

```python
# BUGGY
def trap(height):
    n = len(height)
    if n < 3:
        return 0
    total = 0
    for i in range(1, n - 1):
        left = max(height[:i])
        right = height[i]
        m = min(left, right)
        if m > height[i]:
            total += m - height[i]
    return total
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected `def trap(height):`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def trap(height):
    n = len(height)
    if n < 3:
        return 0
    left, right = 0, n - 1
    left_max, right_max = 0, 0
    total = 0
    while left < right:
        if height[left] < height[right]:
            if height[left] >= left_max:
                left_max = height[left]
            else:
                total += left_max - height[left]
            left += 1
        else:
            if height[right] >= right_max:
                right_max = height[right]
            else:
                total += right_max - height[right]
            right -= 1
    return total

task-0221-longest-consecutive-sequence · Longest consecutive sequence

Prompt 
Write `longestConsecutive(nums)` returning the length of the longest consecutive elements sequence (values differing by 1) in the array, treating the array as an unordered set. O(n) time.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function longestConsecutive(nums) {
  const numSet = new Set(nums);
  let longest = 0;

  for (const num of numSet) {
    if (!numSet.has(num - 1)) {
      let currentNum = num;
      let currentLength = 1;

      while (numSet.has(currentNum + 1)) {
        currentNum += 1;
        currentLength += 1;
      }

      longest = Math.max(longest, currentLength);
    }
  }

  return longest;
}

task-0289-chemical-formula-counter · Chemical formula counter

Prompt 
A chemistry agent counts atoms in a chemical formula. `formula` is a valid string where an element is an uppercase letter followed by zero or more lowercase letters (e.g. 'H', 'O', 'Mg'), an element or parenthesized group may be followed by a positive integer count, and parentheses may nest. Write `count_atoms(formula)` returning a dict mapping each element (string) to its total count, with keys sorted alphabetically. Example: count_atoms('Mg(OH)2') -> {'H': 2, 'Mg': 1, 'O': 2}.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def f(...)`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
```python
def count_atoms(formula):
    stack = [{}]
    i = 0
    n = len(formula)
    while i < n:
        c = formula[i]
        if c == '(':
            stack.append({})
            i += 1
        elif c == ')':
            i += 1
            num_start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            mult = int(formula[num_start:i]) if i > num_start else 1
            inner = stack.pop()
            outer = stack[-1]
            for elem, cnt in inner.items():
                outer[elem] = outer.get(elem, 0) + cnt * mult
        elif c.isupper():
            elem_start = i
            i += 1
            while i < n and formula[i].islower():
                i += 1
            elem = formula[elem_start:i]
            num_start = i
            while i < n and formula[i].isdigit():
                i += 1
            mult = int(formula[num_start:i]) if i > num_start else 1
            outer = stack[-1]
            outer[elem] = outer.get(elem, 0) + mult
        else:
            i += 1
    return dict(sorted(stack[0].items()))
```
Coding · Algorithm Level 2 PASS 6/6 cases

task-0257-k-closest-points-to-origin · K closest points to origin

Prompt 
Write `kClosest(points, k)` returning the `k` points closest to the origin (0,0). Sort by squared Euclidean distance; ties broken by x then y ascending so output is deterministic.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function kClosest(points, k) {
  const dist = (p) => p[0] * p[0] + p[1] * p[1];
  return [...points]
    .sort((a, b) => {
      const da = dist(a);
      const db = dist(b);
      if (da !== db) return da - db;
      if (a[0] !== b[0]) return a[0] - b[0];
      return a[1] - b[1];
    })
    .slice(0, k);
}
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0115-rotate-array-right-by-k · Rotate array right by k

Prompt 
Write `rotate(nums, k)` rotating the array to the right by `k` steps (modular: k may exceed the length). Return the rotated array. Example: `rotate([1,2,3,4,5,6,7], 3)` returns `[5,6,7,1,2,3,4]`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `rotate`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function rotate(nums, k) {
  const n = nums.length;
  if (n === 0) return [];
  const steps = k % n;
  return [...nums.slice(n - steps), ...nums.slice(0, n - steps)];
}

task-0091-merge-strings-alternately · Merge strings alternately

Prompt 
Write `mergeAlternately(word1, word2)` that merges the strings by adding letters in alternating order starting with word1. If a string is longer, append the remaining letters.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `mergeAlternately`.
Model answer 
export function mergeAlternately(word1, word2) {
  let result = '';
  let i = 0;
  const len1 = word1.length;
  const len2 = word2.length;

  while (i < len1 || i < len2) {
    if (i < len1) result += word1[i];
    if (i < len2) result += word2[i];
    i++;
  }

  return result;
}

task-0299-debug-a-memory-leak-in-a-daemon · Debug a memory leak in a daemon

Prompt 
A long-running background daemon's resident memory grows steadily over days until it is killed by the OS. Describe your ordered debugging approach: reproduce the leak in a controlled way, capture heap snapshots at two points in time, compare the snapshots to find what accumulates, attribute the growth to a code path, then confirm the fix. Name the kinds of tools/techniques you would use.
Model answer 
## Ordered debugging approach

**0. Confirm it's a real leak (not noise)**
Before hunting, verify the growth is real and characterize it:
- Track RSS over time via `/proc/<pid>/status` (`VmRSS`), `/proc/<pid>/smaps_rollup`, `ps`, `top`, `vmstat`, `sar`.
- Distinguish a true leak (linear, unbounded) from fragmentation, `mmap`/hugepage growth, thread-stack expansion, or a bounded structure that saturates.
- Check for a correlation with a workload metric (requests, connections, time) — a leak usually scales with work done, not just wall-clock.

**1. Reproduce in a controlled way**
- Drive a realistic workload so growth is observable in minutes, not days: replay production traffic, use a load generator, or run several instances in parallel to compress time.
- Prefer a staging environment mirroring production config (same code, same dependencies).
- Keep the process stable and instrumented throughout so the reproduction matches production.

**2. Capture heap snapshots at two points in time**
- Take snapshot **T1**, let it run a meaningful interval, then snapshot **T2** (gap large enough that signal exceeds allocation noise).
- Tools by language/runtime:
  - **C/C++:** Heaptrack, Valgrind Massif, tcmalloc/jemalloc heap profiler, or ASan/LSan for leak detection.
  - **Java:** `jmap -histo:live`, `jmap -dump:live`, Eclipse MAT, VisualVM.
  - **Go:** `net/pprof` heap profiles (`GODEBUG=gctrace=1`).
  - **Python:** `tracemalloc`, `memray`, `pympler`, `objgraph`.
  - **Node:** `--heapsnapshot` on signal, `clinic`, `heapdump`.

**3. Compare snapshots to find what accumulates**
- Diff T1 vs T2 and rank by **retained (dominator) size**, not just shallow size — retained size shows what's actually kept alive.
- Identify object types/allocations whose count or retained size grew monotonically.
- Tools: Eclipse MAT (dominator tree + histogram), Heaptrack diff, `massif-diff`/`ms_print`, Go pprof `--diff`, Python `objgraph.show_growth`.

**4. Attribute growth to a code path**
- From the accumulating type, follow its allocation **stack traces** to the source location.
- Read the code at that site and look for the classic leak patterns: unbounded cache/map/slice with no eviction, listeners/callbacks never deregistered, resources (sockets, file handles, goroutines) not closed, growing buffers, accidental strong references (closures, caches) preventing GC, thread-local or per-connection state retained.
- Confirm the leaky path is actually exercised by the reproduced workload.

**5. Confirm the fix**
- Apply the fix (add eviction/TTL, close resources, use weak references, bound the collection, fix lifecycle).
- Re-run the **same controlled reproduction** and verify RSS/heap **plateaus** over the same window — this is the key confirmation.
- Run an extended soak test (hours/days) and add it as a regression test.
- Optionally validate in production with eBPF-based monitoring or continuous heap sampling to ensure the leak is gone under real load.

**Supporting techniques:** static analysis (clang-tidy, cppcheck, CodeQL, semgrep), continuous leak instrumentation (ASan/LSan in CI), and differential profiling (T2 − T1) to isolate the delta rather than the cumulative total.
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

```js
export function rotateRight(nums, k) {
  const n = nums.length;
  if (n === 0) return [];
  k %= n;
  const out = new Array(n);
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    out[i] = nums[(i - k + n) % n];
  }
  return out;
}
```

task-0079-roman-to-integer · Roman to Integer

Prompt 
Write `roman_to_int(s)` in Python converting a valid Roman numeral string to an integer.

Output ONLY the function `roman_to_int`.
Model answer 
```python
def roman_to_int(s):
    values = {
        'I': 1,
        'V': 5,
        'X': 10,
        'L': 50,
        'C': 100,
        'D': 500,
        'M': 1000,
    }
    total = 0
    prev = 0
    for ch in reversed(s):
        value = values[ch]
        if value < prev:
            total -= value
        else:
            total += value
            prev = value
    return total
```

import functools
def largest_number(nums):
    def cmp(a, b):
        if a + b > b + a:
            return -1
        elif a + b < b + a:
            return 1
        else:
            return 0
    s = [str(n) for n in nums]
    s.sort(key=functools.cmp_to_key(cmp))
    res = "".join(s)
    if res and res[0] == '0':
        return '0'
    return res

task-0286-tic-tac-toe-game-solver · Tic-tac-toe game solver

Prompt 
A game agent computes the game-theoretic value of a tic-tac-toe position using minimax. `board` is a string of 9 characters ('0'..'8' read left-to-right, top-to-bottom), each 'X', 'O', or '.' (empty). It is X's turn to move, and no one has won yet. Write `ticTacToeResult(board)` returning 1 if X can force a win with perfect play, 0 if the best X can do is a draw, and -1 if O can force a win. X maximizes, O minimizes. Rows/cols/diagonals of three identical marks win.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```js
const LINES = [
  [0, 1, 2], [3, 4, 5], [6, 7, 8],
  [0, 3, 6], [1, 4, 7], [2, 5, 8],
  [0, 4, 8], [2, 4, 6],
];

function checkWinner(board) {
  for (const [a, b, c] of LINES) {
    if (board[a] !== '.' && board[a] === board[b] && board[b] === board[c]) {
      return board[a];
    }
  }
  return null;
}

export function ticTacToeResult(board) {
  const memo = new Map();

  function minimax(b, isX) {
    const winner = checkWinner(b);
    if (winner === 'X') return 1;
    if (winner === 'O') return -1;

    const key = b + (isX ? 'X' : 'O');
    if (memo.has(key)) return memo.get(key);

    let best;
    if (isX) {
      best = -Infinity;
      for (let i = 0; i < 9; i++) {
        if (b[i] === '.') {
          const nb = b.slice(0, i) + 'X' + b.slice(i + 1);
          best = Math.max(best, minimax(nb, false));
        }
      }
    } else {
      best = Infinity;
      for (let i = 0; i < 9; i++) {
        if (b[i] === '.') {
          const nb = b.slice(0, i) + 'O' + b.slice(i + 1);
          best = Math.min(best, minimax(nb, true));
        }
      }
    }

    if (best === -Infinity || best === Infinity) best = 0;

    memo.set(key, best);
    return best;
  }

  return minimax(board, true);
}
```
Reasoning Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0071-two-sum-return-indices · Two Sum — return indices

Prompt 
Write a function `twoSum(nums, target)` that returns the indices of the two numbers that add up to `target`. You may assume exactly one solution exists, and you may not use the same element twice. Return the indices in any order.

Output ONLY the function definition (an ES module exporting `twoSum`), no explanation, no wrapper script.
Model answer 
export function twoSum(nums, target) {
  const map = new Map();
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    const complement = target - nums[i];
    if (map.has(complement)) {
      return [map.get(complement), i];
    }
    map.set(nums[i], i);
  }
  return [];
}

task-0284-build-order-planner · Build order planner

Prompt 
A build agent must order project builds. `projects` is an array of unique project names. `deps` is a list of pairs [a, b] meaning project `a` depends on project `b` (b must be built before a). Write `buildOrder(projects, deps)` returning a valid build order (array of all project names) such that every dependency is built first. If it is impossible (a dependency cycle exists) return -1. When several projects are ready at once, build them in ascending alphabetical order so the result is deterministic.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function buildOrder(projects, deps) {
  const inDegree = {};
  const adj = {};
  for (const p of projects) {
    inDegree[p] = 0;
    adj[p] = [];
  }
  for (const [a, b] of deps) {
    adj[b].push(a);
    inDegree[a]++;
  }
  const ready = projects.filter(p => inDegree[p] === 0).sort();
  const result = [];
  while (ready.length) {
    const node = ready.shift();
    result.push(node);
    for (const next of adj[node]) {
      inDegree[next]--;
      if (inDegree[next] === 0) {
        ready.push(next);
        ready.sort();
      }
    }
  }
  if (result.length !== projects.length) return -1;
  return result;
}
Knowledge Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0004-np-completeness-precise-definition · NP-Completeness Precise Definition

Prompt 
Which statement precisely defines what it means for a problem to be NP-complete?
Model answer 
A problem is **NP-complete** if and only if it satisfies both of the following conditions:

1. **It is in NP** — a proposed solution can be *verified* in polynomial time.
2. **It is NP-hard** — every problem in NP can be *reduced to it* in polynomial time (i.e., it is at least as hard as the hardest problems in NP).

In short: **NP-complete = in NP + NP-hard (polynomial-time reducibility from all NP problems).**

*(Note: No answer choices were included in your message, so I've given the precise standard definition. If you share the specific options, I'll identify the exact matching letter.)*

Model answer 
export function isSubsequence(s, t) {
  let i = 0;
  for (let j = 0; j < t.length && i < s.length; j++) {
    if (s[i] === t[j]) {
      i++;
    }
  }
  return i === s.length;
}

task-0254-stock-with-cooldown-max-profit · Stock with cooldown max profit

Prompt 
Write `maxProfitCooldown(prices)` returning the max profit from trading a stock. You may buy and sell multiple times (one share at a time), but you must wait one day (cooldown) after selling before buying again. State DP.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```js
export function maxProfitCooldown(prices) {
  if (!prices || prices.length === 0) return 0;

  // hold: max profit while currently holding a stock
  // sold: max profit just after selling (entering cooldown)
  // rest: max profit while not holding and not in cooldown
  let hold = -Infinity;
  let sold = 0;
  let rest = 0;

  for (const price of prices) {
    const prevHold = hold;
    const prevSold = sold;
    const prevRest = rest;

    hold = Math.max(prevHold, prevRest - price); // keep holding, or buy from rest
    sold = prevHold + price;                      // sell today
    rest = Math.max(prevRest, prevSold);          // keep resting, or exit cooldown
  }

  return Math.max(hold, sold, rest);
}
```
Coding · Algorithm Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0213-frequency-of-maximum · Frequency of maximum

Prompt 
Write `maxCount(nums)` returning how many times the maximum value appears in the array. Return 0 for an empty array.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function maxCount(nums) {
  if (!nums || nums.length === 0) {
    return 0;
  }
  let max = nums[0];
  for (let i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (nums[i] > max) {
      max = nums[i];
    }
  }
  let count = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (nums[i] === max) {
      count++;
    }
  }
  return count;
}

task-0023-js-left-shift-overflow · JS left shift overflow

Prompt 
```js
console.log(1 << 31);
```
What number does this print? (Note the sign bit.)
Model answer 
`-2147483648`

**Why:** JavaScript bitwise operators work on **32-bit signed integers** (Int32). Shifting `1` left by 31 positions moves the single set bit into the **sign bit** (the 32nd bit):

```
0000...0001  →  1000...0000
```

The bit pattern `1000...0000` is the two's-complement representation of −2³¹, which equals **−2147483648**.

task-0140-burst-balloons · Burst balloons

Prompt 
Write `maxCoins(nums)`. You burst balloons to collect coins; bursting balloon i yields nums[i-1] * nums[i] * nums[i+1] (treat out-of-range as 1). Find the maximum coins you can collect by choosing the burst order.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `maxCoins`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function maxCoins(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  const vals = new Array(n + 2);
  vals[0] = 1;
  vals[n + 1] = 1;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    vals[i + 1] = nums[i];
  }

  const dp = Array.from({ length: n + 2 }, () => new Array(n + 2).fill(0));

  for (let len = 2; len <= n + 1; len++) {
    for (let left = 0; left + len <= n + 1; left++) {
      const right = left + len;
      let best = 0;
      for (let k = left + 1; k < right; k++) {
        const coins = vals[left] * vals[k] * vals[right] + dp[left][k] + dp[k][right];
        if (coins > best) best = coins;
      }
      dp[left][right] = best;
    }
  }

  return dp[0][n + 1];
}
```
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0283-disk-head-scheduler · Disk head scheduler

Prompt 
A storage controller schedules disk requests with the Shortest Seek Time First policy. It starts with the head at track `head` and services the pending `requests` (track numbers). At each step it moves to the nearest pending track; if two tracks are equidistant it picks the smaller track number. Write `diskScheduler(head, requests)` returning the total distance (sum of absolute moves) travelled. All values are non-negative integers.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```js
export function diskScheduler(head, requests) {
  const pending = [...requests];
  let total = 0;
  let current = head;
  while (pending.length > 0) {
    let bestIdx = -1;
    let bestDist = Infinity;
    for (let i = 0; i < pending.length; i++) {
      const dist = Math.abs(pending[i] - current);
      if (dist < bestDist || (dist === bestDist && pending[i] < pending[bestIdx])) {
        bestDist = dist;
        bestIdx = i;
      }
    }
    total += bestDist;
    current = pending[bestIdx];
    pending.splice(bestIdx, 1);
  }
  return total;
}
```
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0075-longest-common-prefix · Longest common prefix

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `longestCommonPrefix`.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function longestCommonPrefix(strs) {
  if (!strs || strs.length === 0) return "";
  let prefix = strs[0];
  for (let i = 1; i < strs.length; i++) {
    while (strs[i].indexOf(prefix) !== 0) {
      prefix = prefix.slice(0, -1);
      if (prefix === "") return "";
    }
  }
  return prefix;
}
```

task-0266-min-candies-distribution · Min candies distribution

Prompt 
Write `candy(ratings)` in Python. Children sit in a row with `ratings[i]`. Each child must get at least 1 candy, and a child with a higher rating than a neighbor must get more candies than that neighbor. Return the minimum total candies. Two-pass left/right arrays.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def candy(ratings):
    n = len(ratings)
    if n == 0:
        return 0
    left = [1] * n
    right = [1] * n
    for i in range(1, n):
        if ratings[i] > ratings[i - 1]:
            left[i] = left[i - 1] + 1
    for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
        if ratings[i] > ratings[i + 1]:
            right[i] = right[i + 1] + 1
    return sum(max(left[i], right[i]) for i in range(n))
Coding · Algorithm Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0262-multiply-large-numbers · Multiply large numbers

Prompt 
Write `multiply(num1, num2)` taking two non-negative integer strings and returning their product as a string without converting the whole thing to a native integer. Use grade-school digit multiplication.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function multiply(num1, num2) {
  if (num1 === "" || num2 === "") return "0";
  if (num1 === "0" || num2 === "0") return "0";

  const n1 = num1.length;
  const n2 = num2.length;
  const result = new Array(n1 + n2).fill(0);

  for (let i = n1 - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    for (let j = n2 - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
      const mul = (num1.charCodeAt(i) - 48) * (num2.charCodeAt(j) - 48);
      const p1 = i + j;
      const p2 = i + j + 1;
      const sum = mul + result[p2];
      result[p1] += Math.floor(sum / 10);
      result[p2] = sum % 10;
    }
  }

  let str = "";
  for (const digit of result) {
    if (!(str === "" && digit === 0)) {
      str += digit;
    }
  }
  return str === "" ? "0" : str;
}
```

task-0130-kth-largest-element-in-array · Kth largest element in array

Prompt 
Write `findKthLargest(nums, k)` returning the kth largest element in the array (1-indexed, the largest is k=1). Return the value; ties count as separate positions. Example: `findKthLargest([3,2,1,5,6,4], 2)` returns 5.

Model answer 
export function findKthLargest(nums, k) {
  const sorted = [...nums].sort((a, b) => b - a);
  return sorted[k - 1];
}

task-0219-merge-intervals · Merge intervals

Prompt 
Write `merge(intervals)` merging all overlapping intervals. `intervals` is an array of `[start, end]` pairs (end inclusive). Return the merged intervals sorted by start.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function merge(intervals) {
  if (intervals.length === 0) return [];

  const sorted = [...intervals].sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);
  const result = [sorted[0].slice()];

  for (let i = 1; i < sorted.length; i++) {
    const last = result[result.length - 1];
    const current = sorted[i];

    if (current[0] <= last[1]) {
      last[1] = Math.max(last[1], current[1]);
    } else {
      result.push(current.slice());
    }
  }

  return result;
}
Reasoning Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0045-complex-truth-teller-logic · Complex truth-teller logic

Prompt 
Three people, each either a knight (always tells the truth) or a knave (always lies). A says: "B is a knave." B says: "C is a knave." C says: "A is a knave." Exactly how many of the three are knaves?
Model answer 
## Analysis

Let me set up the logic. A knight's statements are true; a knave's are false.

- A says "B is a knave" → **A and B have different types**
- B says "C is a knave" → **B and C have different types**  
- C says "A is a knave" → **C and A have different types**

So we'd need all three to be *pairwise different* in type. But with only two types (knight/knave), if A≠B and B≠C, then A=C — which contradicts the requirement that C≠A.

**Checking both cases for A:**
- If A is a knight → B is a knave → C is a knight → A is a knave. ❌ Contradiction.
- If A is a knave → B is a knight → C is a knave → A is a knight. ❌ Contradiction.

## Conclusion

There is **no consistent assignment** — the three statements form a paradox. No number of knaves (0, 1, 2, or 3) can satisfy all three statements simultaneously.

**No solution exists.**
Knowledge Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0098-longest-substring-without-repeating-char · Longest substring without repeating characters

Prompt 
Write `length_of_longest_substring(s)` in Python returning the length of the longest substring without repeating characters.

Output ONLY the function `length_of_longest_substring`.
Model answer 
```python
def length_of_longest_substring(s):
    last_index = {}
    start = 0
    max_len = 0
    for end, ch in enumerate(s):
        if ch in last_index and last_index[ch] >= start:
            start = last_index[ch] + 1
        last_index[ch] = end
        if end - start + 1 > max_len:
            max_len = end - start + 1
    return max_len
```

task-0214-is-perfect-square · Is perfect square

Prompt 
Write `is_perfect_square(n)` in Python returning True if `n` is a perfect square (non-negative integer square), else False. Do not rely on floating point root; use integer arithmetic.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def is_perfect_square(n):
    if n < 0:
        return False
    if n == 0:
        return True
    lo, hi = 1, n
    while lo <= hi:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        sq = mid * mid
        if sq == n:
            return True
        elif sq < n:
            lo = mid + 1
        else:
            hi = mid - 1
    return False

task-0224-house-robber · House robber

Prompt 
Write `rob(nums)` returning the max sum you can steal from non-adjacent houses. `nums[i]` is money in house i; you cannot rob two adjacent houses. DP.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function rob(nums) {
  let prev = 0;
  let curr = 0;
  for (const num of nums) {
    const next = Math.max(curr, prev + num);
    prev = curr;
    curr = next;
  }
  return curr;
}

task-0112-halves-are-alike · Halves are alike

Prompt 
Write `halvesAreAlike(s)`. The string has even length. Return true if the first half and second half contain the same number of vowels (a, e, i, o, u, plus their uppercase forms).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `halvesAreAlike`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function halvesAreAlike(s) {
  const vowels = new Set(['a', 'e', 'i', 'o', 'u', 'A', 'E', 'I', 'O', 'U']);
  const half = s.length / 2;
  const countVowels = (str) => {
    let count = 0;
    for (const c of str) {
      if (vowels.has(c)) count++;
    }
    return count;
  };
  return countVowels(s.slice(0, half)) === countVowels(s.slice(half));
}

task-0239-word-break · Word break

Prompt 
Write `wordBreak(s, wordDict)` returning true if `s` can be segmented into words from `wordDict` (words can be reused). DP.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function wordBreak(s, wordDict) {
  const wordSet = new Set(wordDict);
  const n = s.length;
  const dp = new Array(n + 1).fill(false);
  dp[0] = true;

  for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
    for (let j = 0; j < i; j++) {
      if (dp[j] && wordSet.has(s.slice(j, i))) {
        dp[i] = true;
        break;
      }
    }
  }

  return dp[n];
}

task-0226-max-water-container · Max water container

Prompt 
Write `maxArea(height)` where `height[i]` is the height of a vertical line at position i. Return the maximum area of water that two lines and the x-axis (distance between their indices) can hold. O(n) two pointers.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function maxArea(height) {
  let left = 0;
  let right = height.length - 1;
  let max = 0;
  while (left < right) {
    const h = Math.min(height[left], height[right]);
    const area = h * (right - left);
    if (area > max) max = area;
    if (height[left] < height[right]) {
      left++;
    } else {
      right--;
    }
  }
  return max;
}

task-0271-stack-operator · Stack operator

Prompt 
A data-structure agent runs a stack. It processes a list of `cmds`, each either 'push <value>' or 'pop'. When a 'pop' is processed and the stack is non-empty, the popped value is recorded; if the stack is empty the pop is ignored. Write `stackOps(cmds)` returning the array of popped values in the order they were popped. Values are arbitrary strings without spaces.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function stackOps(cmds) {
  const stack = [];
  const popped = [];
  for (const cmd of cmds) {
    if (cmd === 'pop') {
      if (stack.length > 0) {
        popped.push(stack.pop());
      }
    } else if (cmd.startsWith('push ')) {
      const value = cmd.slice(5);
      stack.push(value);
    }
  }
  return popped;
}

task-0104-sum-of-unique-elements · Sum of unique elements

Prompt 
Write `sumOfUnique(nums)` returning the sum of the elements that appear exactly once in `nums`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `sumOfUnique`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function sumOfUnique(nums) {
  const counts = new Map();
  for (const n of nums) {
    counts.set(n, (counts.get(n) || 0) + 1);
  }
  let sum = 0;
  for (const [n, count] of counts) {
    if (count === 1) sum += n;
  }
  return sum;
}

task-0143-largest-rectangle-in-histogram · Largest rectangle in histogram

Prompt 
Write `largestRectangleArea(heights)` where heights[i] is the height of a bar of width 1. Return the area of the largest rectangle that can be formed within the histogram. O(n) with a stack.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `largestRectangleArea`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function largestRectangleArea(heights) {
  const stack = [];
  let maxArea = 0;
  const n = heights.length;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    while (stack.length > 0 && heights[stack[stack.length - 1]] >= heights[i]) {
      const h = heights[stack.pop()];
      const w = stack.length === 0 ? i : i - stack[stack.length - 1] - 1;
      maxArea = Math.max(maxArea, h * w);
    }
    stack.push(i);
  }
  while (stack.length > 0) {
    const h = heights[stack.pop()];
    const w = stack.length === 0 ? n : n - stack[stack.length - 1] - 1;
    maxArea = Math.max(maxArea, h * w);
  }
  return maxArea;
}

task-0010-cap-theorem-precise-statement · CAP Theorem Precise Statement

Prompt 
State the CAP theorem precisely. Name the three guarantees, and explain which guarantee a CP system sacrifices when a network partition occurs.
Model answer 
## The CAP Theorem

The **CAP theorem** (formulated by Eric Brewer in 2000, later proven by Gilbert and Lynch in 2002) states that a distributed data store cannot simultaneously guarantee all three of the following properties. It can provide at most **two of the three** at any given time:

## The Three Guarantees

1. **Consistency (C):** Every read receives the most recent write, or an error. All nodes see identical data at the same time — a query to any node returns the latest value.

2. **Availability (A):** Every request receives a non-error response, without the guarantee that it reflects the most recent write. The system stays responsive even when some nodes fail.

3. **Partition tolerance (P):** The system continues to operate despite arbitrary message loss or failure to deliver messages between nodes — i.e., it tolerates a **network partition** (nodes can communicate with some peers but not others).

## The Core Claim

Because network partitions are unavoidable in real distributed systems, **P is effectively mandatory**. The theorem therefore reduces to a choice between two modes when a partition occurs:

- **CP:** Consistency + Partition tolerance
- **AP:** Availability + Partition tolerance

(You cannot have CA without P, since a partition will always eventually occur.)

## What a CP System Sacrifices

A **CP system sacrifices Availability** during a network partition. To preserve consistency, it will refuse or block requests it cannot answer with a guaranteed-up-to-date value — returning errors or waiting until the partition resolves. This ensures no node ever returns stale or divergent data, at the cost of some requests being unavailable while the partition persists.
Coding · Bugfix Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0066-modular-exponentiation · Modular exponentiation

Prompt 
Write `mod_pow(base, exp, m)` in Python returning (base^exp) mod m. Use fast modular exponentiation (repeated squaring); do not compute base^exp directly. `m` is positive and exp >= 0. Return the non-negative integer remainder.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def mod_pow(base, exp, m):
    if m == 1:
        return 0
    result = 1
    base = base % m
    while exp > 0:
        if exp & 1:
            result = (result * base) % m
        exp >>= 1
        base = (base * base) % m
    return result

```js
export function isValidParen(s) {
  const st = [];
  const map = { ")": "(", "]": "[", "}": "{" };
  for (const ch of s) {
    if (ch === "(" || ch === "[" || ch === "{") {
      st.push(ch);
    } else if (ch === ")" || ch === "]" || ch === "}") {
      if (!st.length || st[st.length - 1] !== map[ch]) return false;
      st.pop();
    }
  }
  return st.length === 0;
}
```

task-0233-wiggle-subsequence-length · Wiggle subsequence length

Prompt 
Write `wiggleMaxLength(nums)` returning the length of the longest subsequence that alternates strictly up and down (wiggle). Consecutive equal values collapse. Example `[1,7,4,9,2,5]` -> 6.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function wiggleMaxLength(nums) {
  if (nums.length < 2) return nums.length;
  let up = 1;
  let down = 1;
  for (let i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (nums[i] > nums[i - 1]) {
      up = down + 1;
    } else if (nums[i] < nums[i - 1]) {
      down = up + 1;
    }
  }
  return Math.max(up, down);
}
```

task-0285-nested-list-parser · Nested list parser

Prompt 
A data-ingestion agent must parse a nested-list string into actual nested Python lists. The string `s` is valid grammar: `[]` for an empty list, or `[` followed by comma-separated items where an item is either an integer (possibly negative) or another nested list, followed by `]`. Whitespace may appear anywhere between tokens. Write `parse_nested(s)` returning the nested list structure. Example: parse_nested('[1,[2,[3]],4]') -> [1,[2,[3]],4].

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def f(...)`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
```python
def parse_nested(s):
    pos = 0
    n = len(s)

    def skip_ws():
        nonlocal pos
        while pos < n and s[pos].isspace():
            pos += 1

    def parse_int():
        nonlocal pos
        skip_ws()
        start = pos
        if pos < n and s[pos] == '-':
            pos += 1
        while pos < n and s[pos].isdigit():
            pos += 1
        return int(s[start:pos])

    def parse_value():
        nonlocal pos
        skip_ws()
        if s[pos] == '[':
            return parse_list()
        return parse_int()

    def parse_list():
        nonlocal pos
        pos += 1  # consume '['
        skip_ws()
        result = []
        if s[pos] == ']':
            pos += 1
            return result
        while True:
            result.append(parse_value())
            skip_ws()
            c = s[pos]
            if c == ',':
                pos += 1
            elif c == ']':
                pos += 1
                break
            else:
                raise ValueError("Invalid input")
        return result

    return parse_value()
```
Coding · Bugfix Level 1 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0125-word-pattern · Word pattern

Prompt 
Write `wordPattern(pattern, s)` returning true if `s` follows the same pattern, where each letter in `pattern` maps to exactly one word in `s` (bijection, one-to-one both ways). Example: `wordPattern("abba", "dog cat cat dog")` is true.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `wordPattern`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function wordPattern(pattern, s) {
  const words = s.split(' ');
  if (words.length !== pattern.length) return false;

  const map = new Map();
  const seen = new Set();

  for (let i = 0; i < pattern.length; i++) {
    const p = pattern[i];
    const w = words[i];

    if (map.has(p)) {
      if (map.get(p) !== w) return false;
    } else {
      if (seen.has(w)) return false;
      map.set(p, w);
      seen.add(w);
    }
  }

  return true;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 11/11 cases
100%

task-0073-valid-parentheses · Valid parentheses

Prompt 
Write a function `isValid(s)` that returns `true` if the string contains only balanced and correctly nested parentheses brackets of types `()`, `[]`, `{}`, else `false`. Example: `isValid("([{}])") === true`, `isValid("([)]") === false`.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `isValid`.
Model answer 
```js
export function isValid(s) {
  const map = { ')': '(', ']': '[', '}': '{' };
  const stack = [];
  for (const ch of s) {
    if (ch === '(' || ch === '[' || ch === '{') {
      stack.push(ch);
    } else if (ch === ')' || ch === ']' || ch === '}') {
      if (stack.length === 0 || stack.pop() !== map[ch]) {
        return false;
      }
    }
  }
  return stack.length === 0;
}
```

task-0149-valid-sudoku · Valid Sudoku

Prompt 
Write `isValidSudoku(board)` validating a partially filled 9x9 Sudoku board (cells are "1"-"9" or "." for empty). Return true if no row, column, or 3x3 box contains a duplicate digit. It need not be solvable.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `isValidSudoku`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function isValidSudoku(board) {
  const rows = Array.from({ length: 9 }, () => new Set());
  const cols = Array.from({ length: 9 }, () => new Set());
  const boxes = Array.from({ length: 9 }, () => new Set());

  for (let i = 0; i < 9; i++) {
    for (let j = 0; j < 9; j++) {
      const cell = board[i][j];
      if (cell === '.') continue;
      if (cell < '1' || cell > '9') return false;
      const boxIndex = Math.floor(i / 3) * 3 + Math.floor(j / 3);
      if (rows[i].has(cell)) return false;
      if (cols[j].has(cell)) return false;
      if (boxes[boxIndex].has(cell)) return false;
      rows[i].add(cell);
      cols[j].add(cell);
      boxes[boxIndex].add(cell);
    }
  }
  return true;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 3 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0197-edit-distance · Edit distance

Prompt 
Return the minimum number of operations (insert, delete, replace) to convert string a into string b. Example: edit_distance("horse","ros") -> 3, ("intention","execution") -> 5.

```python
# BUGGY
def edit_distance(a, b):
    m, n = len(a), len(b)
    dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)]
    for i in range(m + 1):
        dp[i][0] = i
    for j in range(n + 1):
        dp[0][j] = j
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if a[i - 1] == b[j - 1]:
                dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1]
            else:
                dp[i][j] = 1 + min(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1])
    return dp[m][n]
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected `def edit_distance(a, b):`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
```python
def edit_distance(a, b):
    m, n = len(a), len(b)
    dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)]
    for i in range(m + 1):
        dp[i][0] = i
    for j in range(n + 1):
        dp[0][j] = j
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if a[i - 1] == b[j - 1]:
                dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1]
            else:
                dp[i][j] = 1 + min(dp[i - 1][j - 1], dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1])
    return dp[m][n]
```

task-0291-minimum-window-finder · Minimum window finder

Prompt 
An agent must find the smallest contiguous substring of `s` that contains every character of `t` (including multiplicity). Write `minWindow(s, t)` returning the minimum-length such substring, or '' if no window exists. If multiple windows tie in length, return the one that appears first. Use a sliding window with character counts.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function minWindow(s, t) {
  if (s.length === 0 || t.length === 0 || s.length < t.length) {
    return '';
  }

  const need = new Map();
  for (const c of t) {
    need.set(c, (need.get(c) || 0) + 1);
  }

  const totalNeed = need.size;
  let formed = 0;

  const window = new Map();

  let left = 0;
  let right = 0;
  let minLen = Infinity;
  let minStart = 0;

  while (right < s.length) {
    const c = s[right];
    window.set(c, (window.get(c) || 0) + 1);

    if (need.has(c) && window.get(c) === need.get(c)) {
      formed++;
    }

    while (left <= right && formed === totalNeed) {
      if (right - left + 1 < minLen) {
        minLen = right - left + 1;
        minStart = left;
      }

      const lc = s[left];
      window.set(lc, window.get(lc) - 1);
      if (need.has(lc) && window.get(lc) < need.get(lc)) {
        formed--;
      }
      left++;
    }

    right++;
  }

  return minLen === Infinity ? '' : s.substring(minStart, minStart + minLen);
}
```
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0243-sliding-window-maximum · Sliding window maximum

Prompt 
Write `max_sliding_window(nums, k)` in Python returning an array containing the maximum element for each contiguous window of size `k`. Use a monotonic (deque) queue, O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
```python
from collections import deque


def max_sliding_window(nums, k):
    if not nums or k <= 0:
        return []
    dq = deque()
    result = []
    for i, num in enumerate(nums):
        while dq and dq[0] < i - k + 1:
            dq.popleft()
        while dq and nums[dq[-1]] < num:
            dq.pop()
        dq.append(i)
        if i >= k - 1:
            result.append(nums[dq[0]])
    return result
```
Coding · Implementation Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0139-edit-distance · Edit distance

Prompt 
Write `min_distance(word1, word2)` in Python returning the minimum number of operations (insert, delete, replace) to convert word1 into word2.

Output ONLY the function definition `def min_distance(...)`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def min_distance(word1, word2):
    m, n = len(word1), len(word2)
    dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)]
    for i in range(m + 1):
        dp[i][0] = i
    for j in range(n + 1):
        dp[0][j] = j
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if word1[i - 1] == word2[j - 1]:
                dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1]
            else:
                dp[i][j] = 1 + min(dp[i - 1][j],      # delete
                                    dp[i][j - 1],      # insert
                                    dp[i - 1][j - 1])  # replace
    return dp[m][n]

task-0248-count-palindromic-substrings · Count palindromic substrings

Prompt 
Write `countSubstrings(s)` returning the number of palindromic substrings in `s` (a single character counts as a palindrome). Expand around centers, O(n^2).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function countSubstrings(s) {
  const n = s.length;
  let count = 0;

  function expand(left, right) {
    let c = 0;
    while (left >= 0 && right < n && s[left] === s[right]) {
      c++;
      left--;
      right++;
    }
    return c;
  }

  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    count += expand(i, i);
    count += expand(i, i + 1);
  }

  return count;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 3 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0144-max-points-on-a-line · Max points on a line

Prompt 
Write `maxPoints(points)` where each point is [x, y] (integers). Return the maximum number of points that lie on the same straight line.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `maxPoints`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
function gcd(a, b) {
  a = Math.abs(a);
  b = Math.abs(b);
  while (b) {
    const t = a % b;
    a = b;
    b = t;
  }
  return a;
}

function maxPoints(points) {
  const n = points.length;
  if (n <= 2) return n;

  let max = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    const slopes = new Map();
    let dup = 0;
    let localMax = 0;
    for (let j = 0; j < n; j++) {
      if (i === j) continue;
      let dx = points[j][0] - points[i][0];
      let dy = points[j][1] - points[i][1];
      if (dx === 0 && dy === 0) {
        dup++;
        continue;
      }
      const g = gcd(dx, dy);
      dx /= g;
      dy /= g;
      if (dx < 0 || (dx === 0 && dy < 0)) {
        dx = -dx;
        dy = -dy;
      }
      const key = dx + ',' + dy;
      const count = (slopes.get(key) || 0) + 1;
      slopes.set(key, count);
      if (count > localMax) localMax = count;
    }
    const total = localMax + dup + 1;
    if (total > max) max = total;
  }
  return max;
}

export { maxPoints };
```
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0076-palindrome-number · Palindrome number

Prompt 
Write `is_palindrome(x)` in Python that returns True if the integer `x` reads the same forward and backward, WITHOUT converting to string. Handle negatives (not palindromes).

Output ONLY the function `is_palindrome`.
Model answer 
def is_palindrome(x):
    if x < 0:
        return False
    if x < 10:
        return True

    divisor = 1
    while x // divisor >= 10:
        divisor *= 10

    while x != 0:
        leading = x // divisor
        trailing = x % 10
        if leading != trailing:
            return False
        x = (x % divisor) // 10
        divisor //= 100

    return True

task-0114-longest-palindromic-substring · Longest palindromic substring

Prompt 
Write `longestPalindrome(s)` returning the longest palindromic substring of `s`. If there are multiple of the same length, return the one starting earliest. Example: `longestPalindrome("babad")` returns `"bab"`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `longestPalindrome`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function longestPalindrome(s) {
  if (s.length < 2) return s;

  let start = 0;
  let end = 0;

  for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) {
    const len1 = expandAroundCenter(s, i, i);
    const len2 = expandAroundCenter(s, i, i + 1);
    const len = Math.max(len1, len2);
    if (len > end - start + 1) {
      start = i - Math.floor((len - 1) / 2);
      end = i + Math.floor(len / 2);
    }
  }

  return s.substring(start, end + 1);
}

function expandAroundCenter(s, left, right) {
  while (left >= 0 && right < s.length && s[left] === s[right]) {
    left--;
    right++;
  }
  return right - left - 1;
}
Reasoning Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0306-recover-from-a-bad-commit-in-shared-hist · Recover from a bad commit in shared history

Prompt 
A commit containing secrets was force-pushed and is now in your shared repository's history; you must scrub it without losing legitimate work. Describe your ordered recovery plan: take a full backup of the repo, rewrite history to remove the commit (naming the tool/approach), rotate or revoke the leaked secrets, coordinate with all team members to fetch the rewritten history, and verify the secret no longer appears anywhere.
Model answer 
## Ordered Recovery Plan

**0. Contain the leak immediately (do this in parallel with Step 1)**
The secret is already compromised the instant it was force-pushed and fetched by others. Rotate/revoke the exposed credentials right away — don't wait for the history rewrite to stop active abuse. Notify the team that a breach is under investigation.

**1. Take a full backup (before any destructive operation)**
Create a bare mirror clone as your rollback point:
```
git clone --mirror <repo-url> backup-repo.git
```
Store it offline/securely. Optionally also `git bundle --all` the remote. This is your safety net if the rewrite goes wrong.

**2. Rewrite history to remove the commit**
Use **`git filter-repo`** (the Git team–recommended tool — faster and safer than `git filter-branch`). For a specific bad commit:
```
git filter-repo --commit-callback 'if commit.node == <bad-commit-hash>: commit.drop = True'
```
If you instead want to scrub the secret string from all commits, **BFG Repo-Cleaner** is the simplest option:
```
bfg --replace-text secrets.txt repo.git
```
Then force-push the rewritten refs and clean up:
```
git push --force-with-lease --all
git push --force-with-lease --tags
git reflog expire --expire=now --all && git gc --prune=now --aggressive
```
Delete any remote branches/stashes that still reference the old commit.

**3. Rotate / revoke the leaked secrets**
Revoke the old credentials, issue replacements, and update them in your secrets manager, CI/CD variables, and config files. Confirm the new secrets are not themselves committed.

**4. Coordinate with all team members**
Because history is rewritten, no one can fast-forward — old and new diverge. Instruct everyone to:
- Stop all work immediately.
- Delete their local clone and **re-clone fresh** (cleanest), or `git fetch` then `git reset --hard origin/<branch>`.
- Rebase or recreate any local feature branches off the new history.
- Update forks, CI pipelines, and any cached remotes.

**5. Verify the secret is gone everywhere**
- Search all refs: `git rev-list --all --objects | xargs git grep <secret>` and `git log -p --all | grep <secret>`.
- Check branches, tags, stashes, and reflogs.
- Run secret scanners across history and remote (e.g., **gitleaks**, **truffleHog**, or GitHub/GitLab secret scanning).
- Confirm the remote has no trace of the secret in any blob, and that the backup (Step 1) is the only remaining copy — quarantine or securely destroy it once verified.

**Key principle:** Back up *before* rewriting, rotate *as soon as possible* (the secret is already exposed), force-push with `--force-with-lease` to avoid clobbering others' work, and require a full re-clone since rewritten history cannot be merged into existing clones.
Code Comprehension Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0030-functional-composition-evaluation-order · Functional composition evaluation order

Prompt 
```js
const double = x => x * 2;
const inc = x => x + 1;
const compose = (...fns) => x => fns.reduceRight((acc, f) => f(acc), x);
console.log(compose(double, inc)(5));
```
What number does this print?
Model answer 
**12**

`compose(double, inc)(5)` applies the functions right-to-left: `double(inc(5))` = `double(6)` = `12`.

The `reduceRight` starts with `x = 5`, applies `inc` first (5 → 6), then `double` (6 → 12).

task-0237-median-of-two-sorted-arrays · Median of two sorted arrays

Prompt 
Write `findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2)` returning the median of the two sorted arrays combined, as a number. If even length return the average of the two middle values. O(log(min(m,n))).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function findMedianSortedArrays(nums1, nums2) {
  if (nums1.length > nums2.length) {
    const tmp = nums1;
    nums1 = nums2;
    nums2 = tmp;
  }

  const m = nums1.length;
  const n = nums2.length;
  const half = Math.floor((m + n + 1) / 2);

  let lo = 0, hi = m;
  while (lo <= hi) {
    const i = Math.floor((lo + hi) / 2);
    const j = half - i;

    if (nums1[i] < nums2[j - 1]) {
      lo = i + 1;
    } else if (nums1[i - 1] > nums2[j]) {
      hi = i - 1;
    } else {
      let maxLeft;
      if (i === 0) maxLeft = nums2[j - 1];
      else if (j === 0) maxLeft = nums1[i - 1];
      else maxLeft = Math.max(nums1[i - 1], nums2[j - 1]);

      if ((m + n) % 2 === 1) return maxLeft;

      let minRight;
      if (i === m) minRight = nums2[j];
      else if (j === n) minRight = nums1[i];
      else minRight = Math.min(nums1[i], nums2[j]);

      return (maxLeft + minRight) / 2;
    }
  }

  throw new Error("Input arrays are not sorted");
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0100-duplicate-zeros · Duplicate zeros

Prompt 
Write `duplicateZeros(arr)` that duplicates each zero in the array by shifting the remaining elements to the right (rightmost elements are dropped so the length stays the same). Return the resulting array. Example: `duplicateZeros([1,0,2,3,0,4,5,0])` returns `[1,0,0,2,3,0,0,4]`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `duplicateZeros`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function duplicateZeros(arr) {
  for (let i = 0; i < arr.length; i++) {
    if (arr[i] === 0) {
      arr.splice(i + 1, 0, 0);
      arr.pop();
      i++;
    }
  }
  return arr;
}

task-0269-directory-resolver · Directory resolver

Prompt 
A shell agent navigates a filesystem. Starting at the root directory `/`, it applies a list of navigation `steps` in order. '..' goes up one level (if already at root, it stays at root), '.' does nothing, and any other string is a directory name that is entered. Write `resolver(steps)` returning the final absolute path as a string (e.g. '/a/b'). At the root return '/'. Steps are lowercase directory names and special tokens '..' and '.'.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function resolver(steps) {
  const stack = [];
  for (const step of steps) {
    if (step === '..') {
      if (stack.length > 0) stack.pop();
    } else if (step !== '.') {
      stack.push(step);
    }
  }
  if (stack.length === 0) return '/';
  return '/' + stack.join('/');
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0252-min-arrows-to-burst-balloons · Min arrows to burst balloons

Prompt 
Write `findMinArrowShots(points)` where `points[i] = [xstart, xend]` are balloon horizontal extents (inclusive). An arrow shot at x bursts all balloons containing x. Return the minimum number of arrows to burst all balloons.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function findMinArrowShots(points) {
  if (points.length === 0) return 0;

  // Sort balloons by their end coordinate
  points.sort((a, b) => a[1] - b[1]);

  let arrows = 1;
  let arrowPos = points[0][1];

  for (let i = 1; i < points.length; i++) {
    // If the current balloon starts after the arrow's position,
    // it isn't burst by the current arrow; shoot a new one.
    if (points[i][0] > arrowPos) {
      arrows++;
      arrowPos = points[i][1];
    }
  }

  return arrows;
}

task-0084-find-pivot-index · Find pivot index

Prompt 
Write `pivot_index(nums)` in Python returning the leftmost pivot index where the sum of numbers to the left equals the sum to the right. Return -1 if none. Use 0 for empty sides.

Output ONLY the function `pivot_index`.
Model answer 
def pivot_index(nums):
    total = sum(nums)
    left = 0
    for i, num in enumerate(nums):
        if left == total - left - num:
            return i
        left += num
    return -1

task-0194-product-of-array-except-self · Product of array except self

```js
// BUGGY
export function productExceptSelf(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  const out = new Array(n).fill(1);
  let left = 1;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    out[i] = left;
    left *= nums[i];
  }
  let right = 1;
  for (let i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    right *= nums[i];
    out[i] *= right;
  }
  return out;
}
```

task-0261-count-derangements · Count derangements

Prompt 
Write `derangements(n)` returning the number of permutations of n items in which no element appears in its original position (derangements), computed exactly by DP: D(0)=1, D(1)=0, D(n)=(n-1)*(D(n-1)+D(n-2)). n in [0, 20].

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function derangements(n) {
  if (n === 0) return 1;
  if (n === 1) return 0;
  let prev2 = 1; // D(0)
  let prev1 = 0; // D(1)
  for (let i = 2; i <= n; i++) {
    const curr = (i - 1) * (prev1 + prev2);
    prev2 = prev1;
    prev1 = curr;
  }
  return prev1;
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0218-squares-of-sorted-array · Squares of sorted array

Prompt 
Write `sortedSquares(nums)` where `nums` is sorted ascending (may contain negatives). Return an array of the squares of each number, sorted ascending. Use two pointers for O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function sortedSquares(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  const result = new Array(n);
  let left = 0;
  let right = n - 1;
  let pos = n - 1;

  while (left <= right) {
    const leftSq = nums[left] * nums[left];
    const rightSq = nums[right] * nums[right];
    if (leftSq > rightSq) {
      result[pos] = leftSq;
      left++;
    } else {
      result[pos] = rightSq;
      right--;
    }
    pos--;
  }

  return result;
}
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0297-diagnose-failing-ci-after-a-merge · Diagnose failing CI after a merge

Prompt 
A teammate merged branch `feature-x` into `main`, and now the CI pipeline fails on `main` but passed on both branches before the merge. You suspect a merge conflict that was resolved incorrectly silently. Describe your ordered diagnosis and fix steps, naming the git commands you would run to reproduce, inspect the merge, bisect the problematic change, and confirm the fix with the test suite.
Model answer 
## Ordered Diagnosis & Fix Workflow

### 1. Reproduce the failure locally
```bash
git checkout main
git pull --ff-only origin main
./run-tests.sh        # or whatever the CI test command is
```
Confirm the exact failing test(s) and capture the error. This isolates the problem from CI flakiness.

### 2. Inspect the merge
```bash
git log --oneline --graph -n 15
git log --merges --oneline -n 5          # identify the merge commit SHA
git show <merge_sha>                      # full diff + both parents of the merge
git log -1 --format='%H %P' <merge_sha>   # list parent1 (main) and parent2 (feature-x)
```
Then see what each side contributed and what the merge actually changed relative to pre-merge main:
```bash
git log <merge_sha>^1..<merge_sha>^2     # commits feature-x introduced
git diff <merge_sha>^1 <merge_sha>^2     # diff between the two branches
git diff <merge_sha>^1 <merge_sha>       # net effect of the merge on main
```

### 3. Look for a silently-mangled resolution
```bash
git grep -nE '^(<{7}|={7}|>{7})' <merge_sha>   # leftover conflict markers
```
If none, the resolution likely **dropped or altered logic** — compare the merge's net diff against feature-x's intended commits to spot missing/changed code.

### 4. Bisect the offending change
```bash
git bisect start
git bisect bad <merge_sha>
git bisect good <merge_sha>^1        # main tip before the merge was known-good
git bisect run ./run-tests.sh        # automated; or test manually:
#   ./run-tests.sh && git bisect good   (else)   git bisect bad
git bisect reset                     # returns to original branch
```
This pinpoints the single commit/hunk responsible. If the culprit is the merge resolution itself rather than a feature-x commit, bisect will converge on the merge boundary.

### 5. Fix and confirm
Restore or correct the broken code, then commit:
```bash
git checkout <merge_sha>^1 -- <mangled-file>   # if a file was silently dropped/altered
# or edit the file directly to restore correct logic
git add <file>
git commit -m "Fix incorrect merge resolution in <area>"
```
Confirm with the full suite and history:
```bash
./run-tests.sh          # must pass completely
git log --oneline -5
```
Finally push and let CI run green to validate end-to-end. If the merge resolution is fundamentally broken and unrecoverable, prefer a targeted fix commit over `git revert` of the merge (which would also discard legitimate feature-x work).
Agentic / Tool-use Level 1 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0270-elevator-commute-distance · Elevator commute distance

Prompt 
An elevator agent starts at floor 0 and services a list of floor `requests` one at a time, travelling directly between consecutive floors. Write `elevator(requests)` returning the total number of floors travelled to service all requests in the given order. Each request is a non-negative integer floor.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function elevator(requests) {
  let current = 0;
  let total = 0;
  for (const floor of requests) {
    total += Math.abs(floor - current);
    current = floor;
  }
  return total;
}

```python
# BUGGY
```
```python
def num_islands(grid):
    if not grid:
```

task-0250-decode-ways · Decode ways

Prompt 
Write `num_decodings(s)` in Python. A message of digits is decoded by mapping 1->A ... 26->Z. Return the number of ways to decode the digit string `s` (only digits). Return 0 if none. DP.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def num_decodings(s):
    if not s or s[0] == '0':
        return 0
    n = len(s)
    dp = [0] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 1
    dp[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, n + 1):
        one = int(s[i - 1])
        if one != 0:
            dp[i] += dp[i - 1]
        two = int(s[i - 2:i])
        if 10 <= two <= 26:
            dp[i] += dp[i - 2]
    return dp[n]
Reasoning Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0028-default-mutable-argument-trap · Default mutable argument trap

Prompt 
```python
def add(item, acc=[]):
    acc.append(item)
    return acc
print(len(add('x')), len(add('y')))
```
What two numbers print in order?
Model answer 
**1 2**

The default argument `acc=[]` is created **once** at function definition time, not on each call. Both calls share the same list:

- `add('x')` → `['x']` → length **1**
- `add('y')` → `['x', 'y']` (the same list, now with `'x'` already in it) → length **2**

This is the classic mutable-default-argument gotcha in Python.
