100%

task-0081-product-of-array-except-self · Product of array except self

Prompt 
Write `productExceptSelf(nums)` returning an array such that answer[i] is the product of all elements of nums except nums[i]. Must run in O(n) without using division.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `productExceptSelf`.
Model answer 
```js
export function productExceptSelf(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  const answer = new Array(n);
  answer[0] = 1;
  for (let i = 1; i < n; i++) {
    answer[i] = answer[i - 1] * nums[i - 1];
  }
  let right = 1;
  for (let i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    answer[i] *= right;
    right *= nums[i];
  }
  return answer;
}
```

```python
def longest_palindrome(s):
    def expand(l, r):
        while l >= 0 and r < len(s) and s[l] == s[r]:
            l -= 1
            r += 1
```

task-0267-robot-footprints · Robot footprints

Prompt 
A cleaning robot starts at floor position (0,0) and executes a list of moves, each one of 'N','S','E','W' (one unit each). Write `robotCleaner(moves)` returning the number of DISTINCT grid cells the robot visits, counting the starting cell as visited once.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function robotCleaner(moves) {
  const visited = new Set();
  let x = 0;
  let y = 0;
  visited.add(`${x},${y}`);
  for (const move of moves) {
    if (move === 'N') y += 1;
    else if (move === 'S') y -= 1;
    else if (move === 'E') x += 1;
    else if (move === 'W') x -= 1;
    visited.add(`${x},${y}`);
  }
  return visited.size;
}
```

task-0120-simplify-path · Simplify path

Prompt 
Write `simplifyPath(path)` canonicalizing an absolute Unix-style file path: collapse multiple slashes, resolve "." and ".." (which goes up one level), and produce a path starting with a single "/". Example: `simplifyPath("/a//b/../../c/")` returns `"/c"`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `simplifyPath`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function simplifyPath(path) {
  const stack = [];
  const parts = path.split('/');
  for (const part of parts) {
    if (part === '' || part === '.') {
      continue;
    } else if (part === '..') {
      if (stack.length > 0) {
        stack.pop();
      }
    } else {
      stack.push(part);
    }
  }
  return '/' + stack.join('/');
}
```
Coding · Algorithm Level 1 PASS 12/12 cases
100%

task-0118-decode-string · Decode string

Prompt 
Write `decodeString(s)`. The encoding rule is k[encoded_string] where the text inside the brackets is repeated exactly k times. The input is always valid. Example: `decodeString("3[a]2[bc]")` returns `"aaabcbc"`. Output can be long.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `decodeString`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function decodeString(s) {
  const stack = [];
  let current = '';
  let num = 0;

  for (const ch of s) {
    if (ch >= '0' && ch <= '9') {
      num = num * 10 + (ch.charCodeAt(0) - 48);
    } else if (ch === '[') {
      stack.push(current);
      stack.push(num);
      current = '';
      num = 0;
    } else if (ch === ']') {
      const repeat = stack.pop();
      const prev = stack.pop();
      current = prev + current.repeat(repeat);
    } else {
      current += ch;
    }
  }

  return current;
}
Agentic / Tool-use Level 3 PASS
100%

task-0302-trace-a-slow-request-across-services · Trace a slow request across services

Prompt 
A user request that normally takes 50ms now takes 5 seconds. The request chains through three backend microservices. Describe your ordered cross-service investigation: reproduce the slow request, enable or inspect distributed tracing, examine logs and metrics for each hop, identify the service where latency spikes, find the root cause (e.g. lock contention, a slow query, or degraded dependency), and describe how to confirm the fix.
Model answer 
# Ordered Cross-Service Investigation

## 1. Establish a Baseline & Reproduce

- **Confirm the symptom** against production metrics first (is it *all* requests or a subset? Did it start at a specific time?).
- **Reproduce in a safe environment** — ideally staging with a sanitized copy of the failing input. If production reproduction is required, use a low-volume canary request with a known-good payload.
- **Capture the request identity**: trace ID, request ID, user/tenant, and the exact endpoint + payload. This ID becomes the thread you pull through every service.
- **Check for scope**: is it a specific input, time-of-day, data volume, or tenant? A single slow request vs. a systemic regression changes your hypothesis (data issue vs. code/deployment issue).

## 2. Enable / Inspect Distributed Tracing

- **Verify trace propagation** — confirm the trace ID is injected at the entry point and propagated via headers (e.g., W3C `traceparent`, or B3) across all three services. If instrumentation is missing, add it now.
- **Pull the trace** from your APM (Jaeger, Zipkin, OpenTelemetry, Datadog, etc.) and view the **span tree with per-span durations**.
- **Identify the slowest span** — the service/hop where latency concentrates. Key questions:
  - Is the delay at the **entry span** (in-process work) or a **child span** (downstream call)?
  - Is it **client-side wait** (span shows the call took long) vs. **server-side processing** (the downstream service's own span is long)?
  - Are there **retries, timeouts, or circuit-breaker** activity inflating duration?

## 3. Examine Logs & Metrics Per Hop

For each of the three services, correlate by trace ID and check:

- **Service A (entry)**: inbound request rate, error rate, p50/p95/p99 latency, CPU/memory, thread pool / connection pool saturation.
- **Service B (intermediate)**: same, plus outbound calls it makes.
- **Service C (leaf/dependency)**: same, plus its own dependencies (DB, cache, external API).

Cross-reference with **infrastructure metrics** at the time of regression:
- CPU throttling, memory pressure/GC pauses, disk I/O, network saturation.
- Connection pool exhaustion, thread starvation, queue depth.
- Cache hit-rate drops (a classic cause of a sudden 100× slowdown).

## 4. Localize the Latency Spike

From the trace + metrics, classify the failure location:

| Signal | Likely Location |
|---|---|
| Downstream span is long, upstream span idle-waits | The **called** service |
| Span long but downstream responds fast | **In-process** work in the calling service |
| Multiple retries/timeouts on one hop | **That hop's dependency** is degraded |
| Latency proportional to data size | **Query/index** issue downstream |
| Latency present only under concurrency | **Lock/contention** or resource exhaustion |

## 5. Determine the Root Cause

Drill into the identified service using its slowest span as an anchor:

- **Slow query**: check DB query logs / slow-query log, execution plan, index usage, lock waits. A missing index or a plan change is the most common cause of a sudden jump from 50ms → 5s.
- **Lock contention**: look for blocked transactions, deadlocks, long-held locks, or `LOCK WAIT` events; correlate with concurrency spikes.
- **Degraded dependency**: the leaf service or external API is slow — check its metrics, rate-limiting, or upstream outage.
- **Resource exhaustion**: thread/connection pool saturation, GC pauses, CPU throttling.
- **Cache stampede / cold cache**: sudden miss storm on cache warm-up.
- **Recent change**: correlate with the last deploy, config change, schema migration, or data load — most regressions tie to a recent change.

## 6. Confirm the Fix

- **Apply the targeted fix** (e.g., add/repair index, release/adjust lock, scale the dependency, roll back the bad deploy, warm the cache).
- **Re-run the same reproduction request** and compare the trace — the specific span should return to baseline (~50ms).
- **Validate end-to-end**: p99 latency, error rate, and throughput across all three services return to normal under representative load.
- **Load test / canary** before full rollout to ensure the fix doesn't shift the bottleneck elsewhere.
- **Add regression coverage**: a synthetic monitor on the happy-path latency SLO and a slow-query/trace alert so this is caught automatically next time.
- **Document** the trace, root cause, fix, and detection gap in the postmortem.

---

**Key discipline throughout**: anchor every observation to the **trace ID**, distinguish **client-wait vs. server-processing** time on each span, and correlate latency with **concurrency and data volume** — these three distinctions resolve ~80% of cross-service latency investigations quickly.
Coding · Algorithm Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0220-can-attend-all-meetings · Can attend all meetings

Prompt 
Write `can_attend(intervals)` in Python. `intervals` is a list of `[start, end]` meetings where end is exclusive. Return True if a person can attend all meetings without overlap.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def can_attend(intervals):
    intervals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
    for i in range(1, len(intervals)):
        if intervals[i][0] < intervals[i - 1][1]:
            return False
    return True

task-0005-coffman-conditions-which-is-not-one · Coffman Conditions — Which Is Not One?

Prompt 
Which of the following is NOT one of the four Coffman conditions necessary for deadlock?
Model answer 
The four Coffman conditions necessary for deadlock are:
1. **Mutual Exclusion**
2. **Hold and Wait**
3. **No Preemption**
4. **Circular Wait**

The classic distractor in this question is **Preemption** (the actual condition is *No* Preemption), or sometimes **Starvation**.

**Preemption** — this is NOT one of the four conditions (the correct condition is "No Preemption").

task-0255-longest-mountain · Longest mountain

Prompt 
Write `longest_mountain(arr)` in Python returning the length of the longest subarray that is a mountain: strictly increasing then strictly decreasing, length >= 3. Return 0 if none.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def longest_mountain(arr):
    n = len(arr)
    if n < 3:
        return 0
    longest = 0
    i = 1
    while i < n - 1:
        if arr[i - 1] < arr[i] and arr[i] > arr[i + 1]:
            l = i - 1
            while l > 0 and arr[l - 1] < arr[l]:
                l -= 1
            r = i + 1
            while r < n - 1 and arr[r] > arr[r + 1]:
                r += 1
            longest = max(longest, r - l + 1)
            i = r
        else:
            i += 1
    return longest
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 9/9 cases
100%

task-0119-validate-ip-address · Validate IP address

Prompt 
Write `validIPAddress(queryIP)` returning "IPv4" if it is a valid IPv4 address, "IPv6" if a valid IPv6 address, else "Neither". IPv4: four decimal groups 0-255 with no leading zeros. IPv6: eight groups of 1-4 hex digits separated by colons.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `validIPAddress`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function validIPAddress(queryIP) {
  if (queryIP.includes('.')) {
    const parts = queryIP.split('.');
    if (parts.length !== 4) return "Neither";
    for (const part of parts) {
      if (!/^\d{1,3}$/.test(part)) return "Neither";
      if (part.length > 1 && part[0] === '0') return "Neither";
      const num = Number(part);
      if (num < 0 || num > 255) return "Neither";
    }
    return "IPv4";
  } else if (queryIP.includes(':')) {
    const parts = queryIP.split(':');
    if (parts.length !== 8) return "Neither";
    for (const part of parts) {
      if (part.length < 1 || part.length > 4) return "Neither";
      if (!/^[0-9a-fA-F]+$/.test(part)) return "Neither";
    }
    return "IPv6";
  }
  return "Neither";
}
Agentic / Tool-use Level 3 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0288-min-refuel-stops · Min refuel stops

Prompt 
A car agent must reach a `target` distance with the fewest refueling stops. It starts with `startFuel` units (1 unit of fuel = 1 unit of distance). `stations` is a list of [position, fuel] sorted by position. The car may stop at any station it can reach and take all of that station's fuel. Write `minRefuelStops(target, startFuel, stations)` returning the minimum number of stops needed to reach or pass the target, or -1 if unreachable. When the car can't reach the target, greedily take fuel from the farthest-reachable highest-fuel station so far (max-heap).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function minRefuelStops(target, startFuel, stations) {
  const heap = [];

  function push(v) {
    heap.push(v);
    let i = heap.length - 1;
    while (i > 0) {
      const parent = (i - 1) >> 1;
      if (heap[parent] >= heap[i]) break;
      const tmp = heap[parent];
      heap[parent] = heap[i];
      heap[i] = tmp;
      i = parent;
    }
  }

  function pop() {
    const top = heap[0];
    const last = heap.pop();
    if (heap.length > 0) {
      heap[0] = last;
      let i = 0;
      const n = heap.length;
      while (true) {
        const l = 2 * i + 1;
        const r = 2 * i + 2;
        let largest = i;
        if (l < n && heap[l] > heap[largest]) largest = l;
        if (r < n && heap[r] > heap[largest]) largest = r;
        if (largest === i) break;
        const tmp = heap[i];
        heap[i] = heap[largest];
        heap[largest] = tmp;
        i = largest;
      }
    }
    return top;
  }

  let fuel = startFuel;
  let stops = 0;
  let prevPos = 0;

  for (const [pos, f] of stations) {
    const dist = pos - prevPos;
    while (fuel < dist && heap.length > 0) {
      fuel += pop();
      stops++;
    }
    if (fuel < dist) return -1;
    fuel -= dist;
    prevPos = pos;
    push(f);
  }

  const dist = target - prevPos;
  while (fuel < dist && heap.length > 0) {
    fuel += pop();
    stops++;
  }
  if (fuel < dist) return -1;
  return stops;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0096-group-anagrams · Group anagrams

Prompt 
Write `groupAnagrams(strs)` grouping strings that are anagrams together. Within each group and across groups, order does not matter for correctness but output should be an array of groups (each group an array of strings).

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `groupAnagrams`.
Model answer 
export function groupAnagrams(strs) {
  const map = new Map();
  for (const str of strs) {
    const key = str.split('').sort().join('');
    if (!map.has(key)) {
      map.set(key, []);
    }
    map.get(key).push(str);
  }
  return Array.from(map.values());
}

task-0132-push-dominoes · Push dominoes

Prompt 
Write `pushDominoes(dominoes)` where each character is '.', 'L' or 'R'. Simulate pushing all dominoes until stable: an 'R' pushes '.' to its right, an 'L' pushes '.' to its left; opposing forces of equal strength leave the middle '.' unaffected. Return the final stable string.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `pushDominoes`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function pushDominoes(dominoes) {
    const n = dominoes.length;
    const force = new Array(n).fill(0);

    // Left to right: 'R' pushes rightward, force decays by 1 each step.
    let f = 0;
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        if (dominoes[i] === 'R') {
            f = n;
        } else if (dominoes[i] === 'L') {
            f = 0;
        } else {
            f = Math.max(f - 1, 0);
        }
        force[i] += f;
    }

    // Right to left: 'L' pushes leftward, force decays by 1 each step.
    f = 0;
    for (let i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        if (dominoes[i] === 'L') {
            f = n;
        } else if (dominoes[i] === 'R') {
            f = 0;
        } else {
            f = Math.max(f - 1, 0);
        }
        force[i] -= f;
    }

    let result = '';
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        if (force[i] > 0) {
            result += 'R';
        } else if (force[i] < 0) {
            result += 'L';
        } else {
            result += '.';
        }
    }
    return result;
}
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0277-turnstile-event-simulator · Turnstile event simulator

Prompt 
A metro agent simulates a turnstile. It begins in the LOCKED state. Each event is either 'coin' or 'pass'. A 'coin' unlocks the turnstile (if already unlocked it stays unlocked). A 'pass' while unlocked lets one person through (counts the pass) and locks the turnstile; a 'pass' while locked is ignored (and the turnstile stays locked). Write `turnstile(events)` applying the events in order and returning an array [finalState, passes] where finalState is 'locked' or 'unlocked' and passes is the total number of successful passes.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function turnstile(events) {
  let locked = true;
  let passes = 0;

  for (const event of events) {
    if (event === 'coin') {
      locked = false;
    } else if (event === 'pass') {
      if (!locked) {
        passes += 1;
        locked = true;
      }
    }
  }

  return [locked ? 'locked' : 'unlocked', passes];
}

task-0122-plus-one · Plus one

Prompt 
Write `plusOne(digits)` where each element is a single digit of a large integer (most significant first, no leading zeros except the number 0 itself). Add one and return the resulting digits.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `plusOne`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function plusOne(digits) {
  const result = [...digits];
  for (let i = result.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    if (result[i] < 9) {
      result[i]++;
      return result;
    }
    result[i] = 0;
  }
  result.unshift(1);
  return result;
}

task-0039-venn-three-category · Venn three-category

Prompt 
A magazine survey of 100 readers found 60 read News, 50 read Tech, 40 read Sports, 30 read both News and Tech, 20 read both News and Sports, 15 read both Tech and Sports, and 8 read all three. How many read at least one of the three magazines?
Model answer 
**A. 93**

Using the inclusion-exclusion principle:

|N ∪ T ∪ S| = |N| + |T| + |S| − |N∩T| − |N∩S| − |T∩S| + |N∩T∩S|

= 60 + 50 + 40 − 30 − 20 − 15 + 8

= 150 − 65 + 8 = **93**

```js
export function compress(s) {
  if (!s.length) return "";

    if (s[i] === s[i - 1]) {
```

```js
export function compress(s) {
  if (!s.length) return "";
  let out = "";
  let c = 1;
  for (let i = 1; i < s.length; i++) {
    if (s[i] === s[i - 1]) {
      c++;
    } else {
      out += s[i - 1] + c;
      c = 1;
    }
  }
  out += s[s.length - 1] + c;
  return out;
}
```

task-0225-max-profit-one-transaction · Max profit one transaction

Prompt 
Write `max_profit(prices)` in Python returning the maximum profit from buying one day and selling on a later day. Return 0 if no profit possible.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def max_profit(prices):
    if not prices or len(prices) < 2:
        return 0
    min_price = prices[0]
    max_profit = 0
    for price in prices[1:]:
        if price < min_price:
            min_price = price
        else:
            profit = price - min_price
            if profit > max_profit:
                max_profit = profit
    return max_profit

task-0126-isomorphic-strings · Isomorphic strings

Prompt 
Write `is_isomorphic(s, t)` in Python returning True if the characters in `s` can be replaced to get `t`, as a one-to-one mapping of characters (no two characters map to the same character).

Output ONLY the function definition `def is_isomorphic(...)`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def is_isomorphic(s, t):
    if len(s) != len(t):
        return False
    map_s_to_t = {}
    map_t_to_s = {}
    for c1, c2 in zip(s, t):
        if c1 not in map_s_to_t:
            map_s_to_t[c1] = c2
        elif map_s_to_t[c1] != c2:
            return False
        if c2 not in map_t_to_s:
            map_t_to_s[c2] = c1
        elif map_t_to_s[c2] != c1:
            return False
    return True
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases

task-0123-jump-game · Jump game

Prompt 
Write `canJump(nums)`. You start at index 0, and from index i you can jump up to nums[i] steps ahead. Return true if you can reach the last index.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `canJump`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function canJump(nums) {
  let maxReach = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (i > maxReach) return false;
    maxReach = Math.max(maxReach, i + nums[i]);
    if (maxReach >= nums.length - 1) return true;
  }
  return true;
}

task-0129-largest-number-formed-by-joining · Largest number formed by joining

Prompt 
Write `largestNumber(nums)` arranging the numbers to form the largest possible value when their string forms are concatenated, returned as a string. If the result would start with zeros (all zeros), return "0".

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `largestNumber`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function largestNumber(nums) {
  const sorted = nums.map(String).sort((a, b) => {
    const ab = a + b;
    const ba = b + a;
    return ab < ba ? 1 : ab > ba ? -1 : 0;
  });
  const result = sorted.join('');
  return result[0] === '0' ? '0' : result;
}
```

task-0287-campus-network-cabling · Campus network cabling

Prompt 
A network planner must cable together all campus buildings with minimal total wiring. Each building is a point [x, y]; the cost to connect two buildings is the Manhattan distance |x1-x2| + |y1-y2|. Write `min_span_cost(points)` returning the minimum total cost to connect all buildings so every pair is connected (a minimum spanning tree). Use Prim's algorithm, O(n^2).

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def f(...)`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def min_span_cost(points):
    n = len(points)
    if n <= 1:
        return 0

    INF = float('inf')
    in_tree = [False] * n
    min_dist = [INF] * n
    min_dist[0] = 0
    total = 0

    for _ in range(n):
        u = -1
        best = INF
        for i in range(n):
            if not in_tree[i] and min_dist[i] < best:
                best = min_dist[i]
                u = i
        in_tree[u] = True
        total += best

        ux, uy = points[u]
        for v in range(n):
            if not in_tree[v]:
                d = abs(ux - points[v][0]) + abs(uy - points[v][1])
                if d < min_dist[v]:
                    min_dist[v] = d

    return total
Agentic / Tool-use Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0296-deploy-service-to-staging · Deploy service to staging

Prompt 
You are an ops agent about to release a web service version to the staging environment. Describe the ordered set of steps/commands you run: run the test suite, build the artifact, deploy it to staging, then verify the deployed instance is healthy by checking its health endpoint. Be specific about the commands and their order.
Model answer 
Here is the ordered sequence I'd run, with each step gated on the previous one succeeding (`set -e` semantics). Assumptions: a Git-hosted repo, a containerized service, and a Kubernetes staging namespace.

**0. Pin the version and branch**
```bash
export VERSION="1.4.2-staging"
git checkout main
git pull --ff-only
```

**1. Run the test suite** (must pass before anything is built or shipped)
```bash
npm test          # or: pytest -q
```
Gate: exit code `0` required. If it fails, abort — do not build.

**2. Build the artifact** (produce an immutable, tagged image)
```bash
docker build -t registry.example.com/myservice:${VERSION} .
docker push registry.example.com/myservice:${VERSION}
```
Gate: build and push both succeed. The tag makes the artifact reproducible.

**3. Deploy to staging** (roll out the new image, then wait for rollout)
```bash
kubectl set image deployment/myservice \
  myservice=registry.example.com/myservice:${VERSION} -n staging
kubectl rollout status deployment/myservice -n staging --timeout=300s
```
Gate: rollout reports success. If it fails, roll back:
```bash
kubectl rollout undo deployment/myservice -n staging
```

**4. Verify health of the deployed instance** (probe the health endpoint)
```bash
HEALTH_URL="https://staging.myservice.example.com/health"
curl -fsS --retry 5 --retry-connrefused --retry-delay 5 "$HEALTH_URL"
```
Gate: HTTP `200` with a healthy body (e.g. `{"status":"healthy"}`). If it doesn't respond cleanly, trigger the rollback from step 3.

**Ordering rationale:** tests validate correctness, the build captures a fixed artifact, the deploy makes it live, and the health check confirms the *running* instance (not just the image) is actually serving — each step's success gates the next.

task-0274-run-length-decompressor · Run-length decompressor

Prompt 
A logistics agent decodes a run-length encoded string. Each letter may be followed by a digit count indicating how many times it repeats; if a letter has no digit, it repeats once. Write `decompress(s)` returning the decoded string. Example: 'a3b2c4' -> 'aaabbcccc' and 'ab2c' -> 'abbc'. Counts are positive integers; input is valid.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function decompress(s) {
  let result = '';
  let i = 0;
  while (i < s.length) {
    const letter = s[i];
    let count = '';
    let j = i + 1;
    while (j < s.length && /\d/.test(s[j])) {
      count += s[j];
      j++;
    }
    const n = count === '' ? 1 : parseInt(count, 10);
    result += letter.repeat(n);
    i = j;
  }
  return result;
}

task-0279-bracket-validator · Bracket validator

Prompt 
A syntax-checking agent validates a string containing only the characters '(', ')', '{', '}', '[', ']'. Write `balanced(s)` returning true if the brackets are correctly nested and every opener has a matching closer, else false. Example: '([{}])' -> true, '([)]' -> false.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function balanced(s) {
  const stack = [];
  const map = { ')': '(', '}': '{', ']': '[' };
  for (const ch of s) {
    if (ch === '(' || ch === '{' || ch === '[') {
      stack.push(ch);
    } else if (ch === ')' || ch === '}' || ch === ']') {
      if (stack.length === 0 || stack.pop() !== map[ch]) {
        return false;
      }
    }
  }
  return stack.length === 0;
}

task-0238-burst-balloons-max-coins · Burst balloons max coins

Prompt 
Write `max_coins(nums)` in Python. Balloons numbered 0..n-1 have value in `nums`. Popping balloon i earns `nums[i-1] * nums[i] * nums[i+1]` (out-of-bound balloons are treated as value 1). Return the maximum coins obtainable by popping all balloons. Use DP over intervals.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def max_coins(nums):
    nums = [1] + nums + [1]
    n = len(nums)
    dp = [[0] * n for _ in range(n)]
    for length in range(2, n + 1):
        for left in range(0, n - length + 1):
            right = left + length - 1
            for k in range(left + 1, right):
                val = nums[left] * nums[k] * nums[right] + dp[left][k] + dp[k][right]
                if val > dp[left][right]:
                    dp[left][right] = val
    return dp[0][n - 1]
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0275-grid-pathfinding-agent · Grid pathfinding agent

Prompt 
A delivery agent must find the shortest path across a grid. `grid` is an m x n matrix of 0 (open) and 1 (obstacle). `start` and `goal` are [row, col]. The agent moves up/down/left/right one cell at a time and cannot enter obstacles or leave the grid. Write `shortestSteps(grid, start, goal)` returning the minimum number of moves to reach the goal, or -1 if it is unreachable. (If already at the goal, return 0.)

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function shortestSteps(grid, start, goal) {
  const m = grid.length;
  const n = grid[0].length;
  const [sr, sc] = start;
  const [gr, gc] = goal;

  if (sr === gr && sc === gc) return 0;
  if (grid[sr][sc] === 1 || grid[gr][gc] === 1) return -1;

  const visited = Array.from({ length: m }, () => new Array(n).fill(false));
  visited[sr][sc] = true;

  const queue = [[sr, sc, 0]];
  const dirs = [[-1, 0], [1, 0], [0, -1], [0, 1]];

  while (queue.length > 0) {
    const [r, c, dist] = queue.shift();
    for (const [dr, dc] of dirs) {
      const nr = r + dr;
      const nc = c + dc;
      if (nr < 0 || nr >= m || nc < 0 || nc >= n) continue;
      if (grid[nr][nc] === 1 || visited[nr][nc]) continue;
      if (nr === gr && nc === gc) return dist + 1;
      visited[nr][nc] = true;
      queue.push([nr, nc, dist + 1]);
    }
  }

  return -1;
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0264-number-of-coin-combinations · Number of coin combinations

Prompt 
Write `coinChange2(amount, coins)` returning the number of distinct combinations of coins (order does NOT matter) that sum to `amount`, using unlimited coins. 1D DP: `dp[s] += dp[s - c]` iterating coins outer loop.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function coinChange2(amount, coins) {
  const dp = new Array(amount + 1).fill(0);
  dp[0] = 1;
  for (const c of coins) {
    for (let s = c; s <= amount; s++) {
      dp[s] += dp[s - c];
    }
  }
  return dp[amount];
}

task-0116-sort-colors · Sort colors

Prompt 
Write `sortColors(nums)` that sorts an array containing only 0s, 1s and 2s in one pass using constant extra space (Dutch national flag algorithm). Return the sorted array (all 0s, then 1s, then 2s).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `sortColors`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function sortColors(nums) {
  let low = 0;
  let mid = 0;
  let high = nums.length - 1;

  while (mid <= high) {
    if (nums[mid] === 0) {
      const tmp = nums[low];
      nums[low] = nums[mid];
      nums[mid] = tmp;
      low++;
      mid++;
    } else if (nums[mid] === 1) {
      mid++;
    } else {
      const tmp = nums[mid];
      nums[mid] = nums[high];
      nums[high] = tmp;
      high--;
    }
  }

  return nums;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0102-find-the-difference · Find the difference

Prompt 
Write `findTheDifference(s, t)` where `t` is formed by shuffling `s` and adding one extra lowercase letter. Return the added letter.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `findTheDifference`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function findTheDifference(s, t) {
  let result = 0;
  for (const c of s) {
    result ^= c.charCodeAt(0);
  }
  for (const c of t) {
    result ^= c.charCodeAt(0);
  }
  return String.fromCharCode(result);
}

task-0223-jump-game · Jump game

Prompt 
Write `canJump(nums)` where `nums[i]` is the maximum jump length from position i. Return true if you can reach the last index, else false. Greedy, O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function canJump(nums) {
  let maxReach = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (i > maxReach) return false;
    maxReach = Math.max(maxReach, i + nums[i]);
    if (maxReach >= nums.length - 1) return true;
  }
  return true;
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 1 PASS 9/9 cases
100%

task-0217-hamming-weight · Hamming weight

Prompt 
Write `hammingWeight(n)` returning the number of 1 bits in the 32-bit unsigned binary representation of non-negative integer `n`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function hammingWeight(n) {
  let count = 0;
  n = n >>> 0;
  while (n) {
    n &= n - 1;
    count++;
  }
  return count;
}
Mathematics Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases

task-0068-dice-sum-ways · Dice sum ways

Prompt 
Write `dice_ways(k, s)` in Python returning the number of ordered outcomes of rolling `k` standard six-sided dice (each showing 1 through 6) whose values sum exactly to `s`. Return 0 if the sum is impossible. Use DP over the number of dice.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def dice_ways(k, s):
    if s < 0 or s < k or s > 6 * k:
        return 0
    # dp[j] = number of ways to reach sum j with the dice rolled so far
    dp = [0] * (s + 1)
    dp[0] = 1
    for die in range(1, k + 1):
        new_dp = [0] * (s + 1)
        for j in range(die, min(6 * die, s) + 1):
            for face in range(1, 7):
                if j - face >= 0:
                    new_dp[j] += dp[j - face]
        dp = new_dp
    return dp[s]
Coding · Algorithm Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0241-kth-largest-element · Kth largest element

Prompt 
Write `findKthLargest(nums, k)` returning the kth largest element (1-indexed) in the unsorted array. Use quickselect or a heap; do not fully sort if you can avoid it.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function findKthLargest(nums, k) {
  const arr = nums.slice();
  const n = arr.length;
  const targetIndex = n - k; // 0-indexed position of the kth largest

  function partition(left, right, pivotIndex) {
    const pivotValue = arr[pivotIndex];
    // Move pivot to the end
    [arr[pivotIndex], arr[right]] = [arr[right], arr[pivotIndex]];
    let storeIndex = left;
    for (let i = left; i < right; i++) {
      if (arr[i] < pivotValue) {
        [arr[i], arr[storeIndex]] = [arr[storeIndex], arr[i]];
        storeIndex++;
      }
    }
    // Move pivot to its final place
    [arr[storeIndex], arr[right]] = [arr[right], arr[storeIndex]];
    return storeIndex;
  }

  function quickselect(left, right) {
    while (left < right) {
      const pivotIndex = left + Math.floor(Math.random() * (right - left + 1));
      const pivotIndexFinal = partition(left, right, pivotIndex);
      if (pivotIndexFinal === targetIndex) {
        return arr[targetIndex];
      } else if (pivotIndexFinal < targetIndex) {
        left = pivotIndexFinal + 1;
      } else {
        right = pivotIndexFinal - 1;
      }
    }
    return arr[left];
  }

  return quickselect(0, n - 1);
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 9/9 cases
100%

task-0246-edit-distance · Edit distance

Prompt 
Write `edit_distance(word1, word2)` in Python returning the minimum number of insert, delete, or substitute operations to convert `word1` into `word2`. DP, O(m*n).

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
```python
def edit_distance(word1, word2):
    m, n = len(word1), len(word2)
    dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)]
    for i in range(m + 1):
        dp[i][0] = i
    for j in range(n + 1):
        dp[0][j] = j
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        for j in range(1, n + 1):
            if word1[i - 1] == word2[j - 1]:
                dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1]
            else:
                dp[i][j] = 1 + min(dp[i - 1][j],      # delete
                                   dp[i][j - 1],      # insert
                                   dp[i - 1][j - 1])  # substitute
    return dp[m][n]
```
Reasoning Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0040-scheduling-conflict-deduce · Scheduling conflict deduce

Prompt 
Four tasks A, B, C, D must be scheduled. B must come immediately after A. C cannot be first. D must come before C. What is the number of valid orderings of the four tasks?
Model answer 
**Setting up the problem:**

Since B must come immediately after A, treat "AB" as a single block. Now I have three units to arrange: **[AB], C, D**.

**All 6 arrangements:**

| Arrangement | Result | C first? | D before C? | Valid? |
|-------------|--------|----------|-------------|--------|
| [AB] C D | A B C D | No | No (D after C) | ✗ |
| [AB] D C | A B D C | No | Yes | ✓ |
| C [AB] D | C A B D | **Yes** | — | ✗ |
| C D [AB] | C D A B | **Yes** | — | ✗ |
| D [AB] C | D A B C | No | Yes | ✓ |
| D C [AB] | D C A B | No | Yes | ✓ |

**Valid orderings:** A B D C, D A B C, D C A B

The number of valid orderings is **3**.

task-0070-count-numbers-avoiding-a-digit · Count numbers avoiding a digit

Prompt 
Write `count_no_digit(n, d)` in Python returning how many integers in the range 1..n (inclusive) do NOT contain digit `d` anywhere in their decimal representation. d is an integer from 0 to 9. Use digit DP or a straightforward scan from 1 to n.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def count_no_digit(n, d):
    s = str(n)
    L = len(s)

    from functools import lru_cache

    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def dp(i, tight, started):
        if i == L:
            return 1
        limit = int(s[i]) if tight else 9
        total = 0
        for digit in range(limit + 1):
            if digit == d and (digit != 0 or started):
                continue
            total += dp(i + 1, tight and digit == limit, started or digit != 0)
        return total

    return dp(0, True, False) - 1
Coding · Algorithm Level 1 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0207-target-sum-pair-count · Target sum pair count

Prompt 
Write a function `pairCount(nums, target)` that returns the number of (unordered) index pairs (i, j) with i < j such that `nums[i] + nums[j] === target`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function pairCount(nums, target) {
  let count = 0;
  const map = new Map();
  for (const num of nums) {
    const complement = target - num;
    if (map.has(complement)) {
      count += map.get(complement);
    }
    map.set(num, (map.get(num) || 0) + 1);
  }
  return count;
}
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0298-zero-downtime-service-migration · Zero-downtime service migration

Prompt 
You must migrate a running service from an old cluster to a new one with zero user-visible downtime. Describe the ordered plan: start new instances, verify their health checks pass, drain connections from the old instances, shift traffic at the load balancer, and confirm you have a rollback plan in case the new instances fail.
Model answer 
## Ordered Migration Plan (Zero Downtime)

**0. Pre-flight (before touching traffic)**
- Provision and configure the new cluster; ensure identical code version, config, secrets, and data/state (replication caught up).
- Confirm monitoring, alerting, and logging are in place for both clusters.
- Verify the load balancer (LB) supports the cutover method you'll use (DNS, or LB health-set/weight changes).
- **Define and rehearse the rollback** (see step 8) *before* starting — this is a prerequisite, not an afterthought.

**1. Start new instances (not yet serving production traffic)**
- Launch new instances in the new cluster and register them with the LB, but keep them **out of the active rotation** (or at 0% weight / marked unhealthy to the LB) so no traffic hits them yet.
- Let them boot, initialize, and warm caches.

**2. Verify new-instance health checks pass**
- Confirm health/readiness probes succeed on every new instance.
- Route a small internal probe (synthetic requests) to the new set to validate end-to-end behavior before exposing real traffic.
- Only proceed once all new instances report healthy.

**3. Shift traffic at the load balancer (gradual)**
- Move to a **canary**: send a small percentage (e.g., 5–10%) of traffic to the new cluster and watch error rates, latency, and saturation.
- If stable, ramp in stages (e.g., 25% → 50% → 100%), holding at each step to observe.
- This is the actual cutover point; the LB is the single choke point that makes the shift atomic and reversible.

**4. Drain connections from old instances (after traffic is shifted off them)**
- Once the old cluster is carrying little/no traffic, **gracefully drain**: stop accepting new connections and let in-flight requests finish (respect the LB's `draining` state and connection-drain timeout).
- Never hard-kill instances mid-request — this is what causes user-visible errors.

**5. Decommission old instances**
- After drains complete and old instances are idle, remove them from the LB and shut them down.

**6. Post-migration confirmation**
- Verify 100% of traffic is on the new cluster, error rates are nominal, and capacity/headroom is adequate.
- Keep old instances available (stopped, not deleted) for a grace period.

**8. Rollback plan (must be ready before step 1)**
- **Trigger conditions:** elevated error rate, latency regression, or saturation on the new cluster at any stage.
- **Mechanism:** reverse the LB weights/rotation (shift traffic back to old instances) — fast because old instances were kept healthy and registered until drained.
- **Data compatibility:** ensure the new code is backward-compatible with shared state/databases so traffic can move either way without schema locks.
- **Dry-run the rollback** during pre-flight so the steps are proven, not assumed.

**Key ordering principle:** bring up and verify the new set *before* touching old traffic; shift at the LB (reversible choke point); drain old instances *after* traffic leaves them; and keep rollback possible at every stage.
Reasoning Level 1 PASS
100%

task-0232-minimum-coins-to-amount · Minimum coins to amount

Prompt 
Write `coin_change(coins, amount)` in Python returning the fewest number of coins needed to make `amount` with unlimited coins of each denomination. Return -1 if impossible.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def coin_change(coins, amount):
    INF = amount + 1
    dp = [0] + [INF] * amount
    for i in range(1, amount + 1):
        for c in coins:
            if c <= i and dp[i - c] + 1 < dp[i]:
                dp[i] = dp[i - c] + 1
    return dp[amount] if dp[amount] != INF else -1
Reasoning Level 2 PASS
100%

Model answer 
**B. 1/3**

task-0101-squares-of-a-sorted-array · Squares of a sorted array

Prompt 
Write `sortedSquares(nums)` given a sorted (ascending) array of integers, returning a new array of the squares of each number sorted in ascending order.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `sortedSquares`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function sortedSquares(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  const result = new Array(n);
  let left = 0;
  let right = n - 1;
  let pos = n - 1;

  while (left <= right) {
    const leftSq = nums[left] * nums[left];
    const rightSq = nums[right] * nums[right];
    if (leftSq > rightSq) {
      result[pos] = leftSq;
      left++;
    } else {
      result[pos] = rightSq;
      right--;
    }
    pos--;
  }

  return result;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0109-find-nearest-valid-point · Find nearest valid point

Prompt 
Write `nearestValidPoint(x, y, points)` where each point is [xi, yi]. A point is valid if it shares the same x or y coordinate as (x, y). Return the index of the valid point with the smallest Manhattan distance |x-xi| + |y-yi|; on a tie return the smallest index. Return -1 if no valid point.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `nearestValidPoint`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function nearestValidPoint(x, y, points) {
  let bestIndex = -1;
  let bestDist = Infinity;
  for (let i = 0; i < points.length; i++) {
    const xi = points[i][0];
    const yi = points[i][1];
    if (xi === x || yi === y) {
      const dist = Math.abs(x - xi) + Math.abs(y - yi);
      if (dist < bestDist) {
        bestDist = dist;
        bestIndex = i;
      }
    }
  }
  return bestIndex;
}

task-0080-container-with-most-water · Container with most water

Prompt 
Write `maxArea(height)` where height[i] is the height of a vertical line at i. Return the maximum area of water a container can hold (two lines + x-axis). Use two pointers, O(n).

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `maxArea`.
Model answer 
export function maxArea(height) {
  let left = 0;
  let right = height.length - 1;
  let max = 0;
  while (left < right) {
    const h = Math.min(height[left], height[right]);
    const area = h * (right - left);
    if (area > max) max = area;
    if (height[left] < height[right]) {
      left++;
    } else {
      right--;
    }
  }
  return max;
}

task-0082-move-zeroes · Move zeroes

Prompt 
Write `moveZeroes(nums)` that moves all zeroes to the end while maintaining the relative order of non-zero elements, in place, and returns nums.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `moveZeroes`.
Model answer 
```js
export function moveZeroes(nums) {
  let lastNonZero = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    if (nums[i] !== 0) {
      const temp = nums[i];
      nums[i] = nums[lastNonZero];
      nums[lastNonZero] = temp;
      lastNonZero++;
    }
  }
  return nums;
}
```

task-0128-repeated-dna-sequences · Repeated DNA sequences

Prompt 
Write `findRepeatedDnaSequences(s)` returning all 10-letter-long substrings that occur more than once in the DNA string. Return them sorted lexicographically. Example: `findRepeatedDnaSequences("AAAAACCCCCAAAAACCCCCCAAAAAGGGTTT")` returns `["AAAAACCCCC","CCCCCAAAAA"]`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `findRepeatedDnaSequences`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function findRepeatedDnaSequences(s) {
  const seen = new Set();
  const repeated = new Set();
  for (let i = 0; i + 10 <= s.length; i++) {
    const sub = s.slice(i, i + 10);
    if (seen.has(sub)) {
      repeated.add(sub);
    } else {
      seen.add(sub);
    }
  }
  return [...repeated].sort();
}
```

```js
  const mid = Math.floor(merged.length / 2);
  return merged[mid];
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `findMedian`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function findMedian(a, b) {
  const merged = a.concat(b).sort((x, y) => x - y);
  const len = merged.length;
  const mid = Math.floor(len / 2);
  if (len % 2 === 0) {
    return (merged[mid - 1] + merged[mid]) / 2;
  }
  return merged[mid];
}

task-0064-arrangements-of-mississippi · Arrangements of MISSISSIPPI

Prompt 
How many distinct arrangements of the 11 letters of the word MISSISSIPPI are there?
Model answer 
**34650**

The word MISSISSIPPI has 11 letters with the following frequencies:
- M: 1
- I: 4
- S: 4
- P: 2

The number of distinct arrangements is the multinomial coefficient:

$$\frac{11!}{1! \cdot 4! \cdot 4! \cdot 2!} = \frac{39916800}{1 \cdot 24 \cdot 24 \cdot 2} = \frac{39916800}{1152} = 34650$$

task-0234-min-subarray-length-sum-target · Min subarray length sum >= target

Prompt 
Write `minSubArrayLen(target, nums)` returning the minimal length of a contiguous subarray whose sum is >= `target`, or 0 if none. Sliding window, O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function minSubArrayLen(target, nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  let minLen = Infinity;
  let sum = 0;
  let left = 0;

  for (let right = 0; right < n; right++) {
    sum += nums[right];
    while (sum >= target) {
      minLen = Math.min(minLen, right - left + 1);
      sum -= nums[left];
      left++;
    }
  }

  return minLen === Infinity ? 0 : minLen;
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases
100%

task-0222-task-scheduler · Task scheduler

Prompt 
Write `least_interval(tasks, n)` in Python. `tasks` is a list of letters where each letter is a task type. Return the least number of unit time slots needed so the same task type is separated by at least `n` units of idle time.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
def least_interval(tasks, n):
    from collections import Counter
    counts = Counter(tasks)
    max_freq = max(counts.values())
    max_count = sum(1 for v in counts.values() if v == max_freq)
    return max(len(tasks), (max_freq - 1) * (n + 1) + max_count)
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 5/5 cases
100%

Model answer 
def restore_string(s, indices):
    result = [''] * len(s)
    for i, c in enumerate(s):
        result[indices[i]] = c
    return ''.join(result)

task-0210-sorted-array-intersection · Sorted array intersection

task-0113-left-and-right-sum-differences · Left and right sum differences

Prompt 
Write `leftRigthDifference(nums)` returning an array where element i is the absolute difference between the sum of elements to the left of i and the sum of elements to the right of i (use 0 for empty sides).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `leftRigthDifference`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function leftRigthDifference(nums) {
  const total = nums.reduce((sum, n) => sum + n, 0);
  const result = [];
  let leftSum = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    const rightSum = total - leftSum - nums[i];
    result.push(Math.abs(leftSum - rightSum));
    leftSum += nums[i];
  }
  return result;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 3 PASS 5/5 cases
100%

task-0138-lru-cache-operation-sequence · LRU cache (operation sequence)

Prompt 
Write `lruCache(capacity, operations)` implementing an LRU (least recently used) cache. Each operation is either ["get", key] or ["put", key, value]. get returns the value or -1 if absent (and marks it most recently used). put inserts/updates (marking most recently used), evicting the least recently used key when over capacity. Return an array of the results in order, using null for put operations.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `lruCache`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function lruCache(capacity, operations) {
  const cache = new Map();
  const results = [];

  for (const op of operations) {
    if (op[0] === "get") {
      const key = op[1];
      if (cache.has(key)) {
        const value = cache.get(key);
        cache.delete(key);
        cache.set(key, value);
        results.push(value);
      } else {
        results.push(-1);
      }
    } else if (op[0] === "put") {
      const key = op[1];
      const value = op[2];
      if (cache.has(key)) {
        cache.delete(key);
      }
      if (capacity > 0) {
        cache.set(key, value);
        while (cache.size > capacity) {
          const lruKey = cache.keys().next().value;
          cache.delete(lruKey);
        }
      }
      results.push(null);
    }
  }

  return results;
}
```
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0253-max-profit-job-scheduling · Max profit job scheduling

Prompt 
Write `job_scheduling(startTime, endTime, profit)` in Python. Job i runs from startTime[i] to endTime[i] (exclusive end) and pays profit[i]. You can choose non-overlapping jobs. Return the maximum profit. Sort by end, DP with binary search.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
```python
def job_scheduling(startTime, endTime, profit):
    import bisect
    n = len(startTime)
    jobs = sorted(zip(startTime, endTime, profit), key=lambda x: x[1])
    end_times = [job[1] for job in jobs]
    dp = [0] * (n + 1)
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        start, end, prof = jobs[i - 1]
        idx = bisect.bisect_right(end_times, start, 0, i - 1)
        include = prof + dp[idx]
        exclude = dp[i - 1]
        dp[i] = max(include, exclude)
    return dp[n]
```
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0124-house-robber · House robber

Prompt 
Write `rob(nums)` returning the maximum amount you can steal from non-adjacent houses, where nums[i] is the money in house i (you cannot rob two adjacent houses).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `rob`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function rob(nums) {
  let prev = 0;
  let curr = 0;
  for (const num of nums) {
    const next = Math.max(curr, prev + num);
    prev = curr;
    curr = next;
  }
  return curr;
}
```

Model answer 
Here's an ordered onboarding sequence. I'll use a Node.js/TypeScript stack as the working example, since the flow is representative — swap the package manager and commands for whatever your repo actually uses (Python: `pip install -r requirements.txt` / `poetry install`; Go: `go mod download`; etc.).

**0. Read the README first**
Before anything else, skim the repo's `README.md` (and any `CONTRIBUTING.md`). It usually lists stack-specific prerequisites, exact commands, and gotchas. This step can save you from guessing wrong on steps 1–6.

**1. Verify prerequisites are installed**


**3. Install dependencies**

**4. Set up environment configuration**

```bash

```

**5. Build (if the project requires it)**
```bash
npm run build
```
Skip if the app runs directly from source (e.g. via `ts-node`/`nodemon`).

**6. Run the test suite to verify things work**
```bash
npm test                   # unit + integration
# often: npm run test:coverage or npm run lint
```
Confirm these pass before proceeding — a green suite is your baseline that the local setup is correct.

**7. Start the app**
```bash
npm start                  # production build
# or for dev: npm run dev
```
Then verify it's actually serving — hit the health/landing endpoint (e.g. `curl http://localhost:3000`) and check the logs for a clean startup with no errors.

**Tip:** If any step fails, the README, CI workflow files (`.github/workflows/`), or a `Makefile`/`justfile` are the best sources of the canonical commands.

task-0074-climbing-stairs · Climbing stairs

Prompt 
You are climbing a staircase. It takes `n` steps to reach the top. Each time you can climb 1 or 2 steps. Write `climbStairs(n)` returning the number of distinct ways to reach the top. `n` is between 1 and 45.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `climbStairs`.
Model answer 
export function climbStairs(n) {
  if (n <= 2) return n;
  let a = 1, b = 2;
  for (let i = 3; i <= n; i++) {
    const c = a + b;
    a = b;
    b = c;
  }
  return b;
}

task-0131-fraction-to-recurring-decimal · Fraction to recurring decimal

Prompt 
Write `fractionToDecimal(numerator, denominator)` returning the decimal representation of the fraction as a string. Enclose the repeating part in parentheses. Example: `fractionToDecimal(4, 333)` returns `"0.(012)"`. Denominator is never 0.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `fractionToDecimal`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function fractionToDecimal(numerator, denominator) {
  const num = BigInt(numerator);
  const den = BigInt(denominator);

  if (num === 0n) return "0";

  let result = "";
  if ((num < 0n) !== (den < 0n)) {
    result += "-";
  }

  const a = num < 0n ? -num : num;
  const b = den < 0n ? -den : den;

  result += (a / b).toString();

  let remainder = a % b;
  if (remainder === 0n) {
    return result;
  }

  result += ".";

  const map = new Map();
  let frac = "";
  while (remainder !== 0n) {
    if (map.has(remainder)) {
      const idx = map.get(remainder);
      frac = frac.slice(0, idx) + "(" + frac.slice(idx) + ")";
      break;
    }
    map.set(remainder, frac.length);
    remainder *= 10n;
    frac += (remainder / b).toString();
    remainder %= b;
  }

  return result + frac;
}
Mathematics Level 2 PASS
100%

task-0294-minimum-jumps-agent · Minimum jumps agent

Prompt 
A parkour agent at position 0 must reach the last index. At index i, `nums[i]` is the maximum number of steps it can jump forward (any integer 0..nums[i]). The last index is always reachable. Write `jumpGameII(nums)` returning the minimum number of jumps to reach the last index. If already at the last index (single element), return 0. Use a greedy BFS over reachable ranges.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function jumpGameII(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  if (n <= 1) return 0;

  let jumps = 0;
  let currentEnd = 0;
  let farthest = 0;

  for (let i = 0; i < n - 1; i++) {
    farthest = Math.max(farthest, i + nums[i]);
    if (i === currentEnd) {
      jumps++;
      currentEnd = farthest;
      if (currentEnd >= n - 1) break;
    }
  }

  return jumps;
}
Coding · Algorithm Level 3 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0244-split-array-largest-sum · Split array largest sum

Prompt 
Write `splitArray(nums, k)` returning the minimum possible largest sum among `k` contiguous subarrays when partitioning `nums`. Use binary search on the answer with a feasibility check, O(n log(sum)).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function splitArray(nums, k) {
  let lo = Math.max(...nums);
  let hi = nums.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);

  function feasible(limit) {
    let parts = 1;
    let current = 0;
    for (const num of nums) {
      if (current + num > limit) {
        parts++;
        current = num;
        if (parts > k) return false;
      } else {
        current += num;
      }
    }
    return true;
  }

  while (lo < hi) {
    const mid = Math.floor((lo + hi) / 2);
    if (feasible(mid)) {
      hi = mid;
    } else {
      lo = mid + 1;
    }
  }

  return lo;
}
```
Agentic / Tool-use Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0278-token-bucket-rate-limiter · Token bucket rate limiter

Prompt 
An API gateway uses a token bucket to rate-limit requests. The bucket starts full with `capacity` tokens and refills continuously at `refillPerSec` tokens per second, capped at `capacity`. `events` is a time-ordered list of [timeSeconds, cost]; process events in order: first refill using the elapsed time since the previous event (the first event's elapsed time starts from 0), then if at least `cost` tokens are available deduct them (request allowed, record true) else the request is denied (record false). Fractional tokens are allowed. Write `tokenBucket(events, capacity, refillPerSec)` returning an array of booleans, one per event.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function tokenBucket(events, capacity, refillPerSec) {
  let tokens = capacity;
  let prevTime = 0;
  const results = [];

  for (const [time, cost] of events) {
    const elapsed = time - prevTime;
    tokens = Math.min(capacity, tokens + elapsed * refillPerSec);
    prevTime = time;

    if (tokens >= cost) {
      tokens -= cost;
      results.push(true);
    } else {
      results.push(false);
    }
  }

  return results;
}
```
Coding · Bugfix Level 3 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0067-3x3-determinant · 3x3 determinant

Prompt 
Write `det3(mat)` in Python that computes and returns the determinant of a 3x3 integer matrix supplied as a list of 3 rows (each a list of 3 integers). Return the exact integer determinant.

Output ONLY the function definition in Python (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no import statements other than stdlib, no main block, no tests.
Model answer 
def det3(mat):
    a, b, c = mat[0]
    d, e, f = mat[1]
    g, h, i = mat[2]
    return a * (e * i - f * h) - b * (d * i - f * g) + c * (d * h - e * g)

task-0183-roman-to-integer · Roman to integer

Prompt 
Convert a valid Roman numeral string to an integer, handling subtractive forms (IV=4, IX=9, XL=40, etc.).

```js
// BUGGY
export function romanToInt(s) {
  const v = { I: 1, V: 5, X: 10, L: 50, C: 100, D: 500, M: 1000 };
  let total = 0;
  for (const ch of s) total += v[ch];
  return total;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `romanToInt`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
export function romanToInt(s) {
  const v = { I: 1, V: 5, X: 10, L: 50, C: 100, D: 500, M: 1000 };
  let total = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) {
    const cur = v[s[i]];
    const next = v[s[i + 1]];
    if (next !== undefined && cur < next) {
      total += next - cur;
      i++;
    } else {
      total += cur;
    }
  }
  return total;
}

task-0105-ransom-note · Ransom note

Prompt 
Write `canConstruct(ransomNote, magazine)` returning true if `ransomNote` can be constructed using the letters from `magazine` (each letter in magazine used at most once).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `canConstruct`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function canConstruct(ransomNote, magazine) {
  const counts = new Map();
  for (const ch of magazine) {
    counts.set(ch, (counts.get(ch) || 0) + 1);
  }
  for (const ch of ransomNote) {
    if ((counts.get(ch) || 0) <= 0) {
      return false;
    }
    counts.set(ch, counts.get(ch) - 1);
  }
  return true;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 1 PASS 6/6 cases

task-0089-kids-with-greatest-candies · Kids with greatest candies

Prompt

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `kidsWithCandies`.
Model answer 
export function kidsWithCandies(candies, extra) {
  const max = Math.max(...candies);
  return candies.map((c) => c + extra >= max);
}

task-0200-largest-rectangle-in-histogram · Largest rectangle in histogram

Prompt 
Given bar heights of a histogram (width 1 each), return the area of the largest rectangle that can be formed. Example: [2,1,5,6,2,3] -> 10.

```js
// BUGGY
export function largestRectangle(heights) {
  let max = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < heights.length; i++) {
    let h = heights[i];
    for (let j = i; j < heights.length; j++) {
      h = Math.min(h, heights[j]);
      max = Math.max(max, h * (j - i));
    }
  }
  return max;
}
```

    let h = heights[i];
    for (let j = i; j < heights.length; j++) {
      h = Math.min(h, heights[j]);

task-0093-reverse-vowels-of-a-string · Reverse vowels of a string

Prompt 
Write `reverseVowels(s)` that reverses only the vowels (a,e,i,o,u, both cases) in the string, leaving all other characters in place.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `reverseVowels`.
Model answer 
export function reverseVowels(s) {
  const vowels = new Set(['a', 'e', 'i', 'o', 'u', 'A', 'E', 'I', 'O', 'U']);
  const chars = s.split('');
  let left = 0;
  let right = chars.length - 1;
  while (left < right) {
    while (left < right && !vowels.has(chars[left])) left++;
    while (left < right && !vowels.has(chars[right])) right--;
    if (left < right) {
      const temp = chars[left];
      chars[left] = chars[right];
      chars[right] = temp;
      left++;
      right--;
    }
  }
  return chars.join('');
}

100%

task-0034-basic-logical-converse · Basic logical converse

Prompt 
Which of the following is logically equivalent to "If it is raining, then the ground is wet"?
Model answer 
The statement "If it is raining, then the ground is wet" is a conditional of the form **P → Q**.

The logically equivalent statement is its **contrapositive**:

**"If the ground is not wet, then it is not raining."**

(¬Q → ¬P)

Note: The converse ("If the ground is wet, then it is raining") and the inverse ("If it is not raining, then the ground is not wet") are **NOT** logically equivalent.

Prompt 
Write `runningSum(nums)` returning an array where each element is the cumulative sum of nums up to that index.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `runningSum`.
Model answer 
export function runningSum(nums) {
  const result = [];
  let sum = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < nums.length; i++) {
    sum += nums[i];
    result.push(sum);
  }
  return result;
}

task-0134-trapping-rain-water · Trapping rain water

Prompt 
Write `trap(height)` where height[i] is the height of a bar. Compute how much water it can trap after rain. Example: `trap([0,1,0,2,1,0,1,3,2,1,2,1])` returns 6.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `trap`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function trap(height) {
  const n = height.length;
  if (n === 0) return 0;

  const leftMax = new Array(n);
  const rightMax = new Array(n);

  leftMax[0] = height[0];
  for (let i = 1; i < n; i++) {
    leftMax[i] = Math.max(leftMax[i - 1], height[i]);
  }

  rightMax[n - 1] = height[n - 1];
  for (let i = n - 2; i >= 0; i--) {
    rightMax[i] = Math.max(rightMax[i + 1], height[i]);
  }

  let water = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    water += Math.min(leftMax[i], rightMax[i]) - height[i];
  }

  return water;
}
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 8/8 cases
100%

task-0133-max-consecutive-ones-with-one-flip · Max consecutive ones with one flip

Prompt 
Write `find_max_consecutive_ones(nums)` in Python. The binary array contains 0s and 1s. You may flip at most one 0 to 1. Return the length of the longest subarray of 1s achievable.

Output ONLY the function definition `def find_max_consecutive_ones(...)`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
def find_max_consecutive_ones(nums):
    left = 0
    zeros = 0
    max_len = 0
    for right in range(len(nums)):
        if nums[right] == 0:
            zeros += 1
        while zeros > 1:
            if nums[left] == 0:
                zeros -= 1
            left += 1
        max_len = max(max_len, right - left + 1)
    return max_len
Coding · Algorithm Level 1 PASS 8/8 cases

task-0211-sum-of-digits · Sum of digits

Prompt 
Write `sumDigits(n)` returning the sum of the decimal digits of non-negative integer `n`. Example: `sumDigits(1234) === 10`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function sumDigits(n) {
  let sum = 0;
  const str = String(Math.abs(n));
  for (const ch of str) {
    if (ch >= '0' && ch <= '9') {
      sum += ch.charCodeAt(0) - 48;
    }
  }
  return sum;
}

task-0094-increasing-triplet-subsequence · Increasing triplet subsequence

Prompt 
Write `increasingTriplet(nums)` returning true if there exist i < j < k where nums[i] < nums[j] < nums[k]. O(n) time, O(1) space.

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `increasingTriplet`.
Model answer 
export function increasingTriplet(nums) {
  let first = Infinity;
  let second = Infinity;
  for (const num of nums) {
    if (num <= first) {
      first = num;
    } else if (num <= second) {
      second = num;
    } else {
      return true;
    }
  }
  return false;
}

task-0146-remove-duplicate-letters-smallest-lexico · Remove duplicate letters (smallest lexicographic)

Prompt 
Write `removeDuplicateLetters(s)` returning the smallest lexicographically result you can obtain by removing duplicate letters (each letter must appear exactly once, order corresponds to remaining character order). Example: `removeDuplicateLetters("bcabc")` returns `"abc"`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `removeDuplicateLetters`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function removeDuplicateLetters(s) {
  const count = new Array(26).fill(0);
  for (const c of s) {
    count[c.charCodeAt(0) - 97]++;
  }
  const visited = new Array(26).fill(false);
  const stack = [];
  for (const c of s) {
    const idx = c.charCodeAt(0) - 97;
    count[idx]--;
    if (visited[idx]) continue;
    while (
      stack.length > 0 &&
      stack[stack.length - 1] > c &&
      count[stack[stack.length - 1].charCodeAt(0) - 97] > 0
    ) {
      const top = stack.pop();
      visited[top.charCodeAt(0) - 97] = false;
    }
    stack.push(c);
    visited[idx] = true;
  }
  return stack.join('');
}
```
Coding · Implementation Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0117-next-permutation · Next permutation

Prompt 
Write `nextPermutation(nums)` returning the next lexicographic permutation of the array (as a new array). If it's already the greatest permutation, wrap around to the smallest (ascending). Example: `nextPermutation([1,2,3])` returns `[1,3,2]`.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `nextPermutation`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```javascript
export function nextPermutation(nums) {
  const arr = [...nums];
  const n = arr.length;

  let i = n - 2;
  while (i >= 0 && arr[i] >= arr[i + 1]) {
    i--;
  }

  if (i >= 0) {
    let j = n - 1;
    while (arr[j] <= arr[i]) {
      j--;
    }
    [arr[i], arr[j]] = [arr[j], arr[i]];
  }

  let left = i + 1;
  let right = n - 1;
  while (left < right) {
    [arr[left], arr[right]] = [arr[right], arr[left]];
    left++;
    right--;
  }

  return arr;
}
```
Coding · Implementation Level 3 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0141-candy-distribution · Candy distribution

Prompt 
Write `candy(ratings)` returning the minimum candies needed so that every child gets at least one, and a child with a higher rating than a neighbor gets strictly more candies than that neighbor.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `candy`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
export function candy(ratings) {
  const n = ratings.length;
  if (n === 0) return 0;
  const candies = new Array(n).fill(1);

  for (let i = 1; i < n; i++) {
    if (ratings[i] > ratings[i - 1]) {
      candies[i] = candies[i - 1] + 1;
    }
  }

  for (let i = n - 2; i >= 0; i--) {
    if (ratings[i] > ratings[i + 1]) {
      candies[i] = Math.max(candies[i], candies[i + 1] + 1);
    }
  }

  return candies.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
}
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%

task-0193-happy-number · Happy number

Prompt 
Return true if n is a happy number: repeatedly replace n with the sum of the squares of its digits, until it becomes 1 (true) or cycles forever (false). Example: 19 -> true, 2 -> false.

```js
// BUGGY
export function isHappy(n) {
  const seen = new Set();
  while (n !== 1) {
    let s = 0;
    while (n > 0) {
      const d = n % 10;
      s += d * d;
      n = Math.floor(n / 10);
    }
    n = s;
  }
  return true;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `isHappy`. No markdown fences, no explanation.
Model answer 
```js
export function isHappy(n) {
  const seen = new Set();
  while (n !== 1 && !seen.has(n)) {
    seen.add(n);
    let s = 0;
    while (n > 0) {
      const d = n % 10;
      s += d * d;
      n = Math.floor(n / 10);
    }
    n = s;
  }
  return n === 1;
}
```

task-0229-daily-temperatures · Daily temperatures

Prompt 
Write `dailyTemperatures(temperatures)` returning an array where answer[i] is the number of days until a warmer temperature (strictly greater), or 0 if no warmer day comes. Monotonic stack, O(n).

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function ...`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function dailyTemperatures(temperatures) {
  const n = temperatures.length;
  const answer = new Array(n).fill(0);
  const stack = []; // indices of days waiting for a warmer temperature

  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    while (stack.length && temperatures[i] > temperatures[stack[stack.length - 1]]) {
      const prev = stack.pop();
      answer[prev] = i - prev;
    }
    stack.push(i);
  }

  return answer;
}

task-0198-coin-change-min-coins · Coin change (min coins)

```python
# BUGGY
def coin_change(coins, amount):
    INF = float("inf")
    dp = [INF] * (amount + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    for a in range(amount + 1):
        for c in coins:
            if a >= c:
                dp[a] = min(dp[a], dp[a - c])
    return dp[amount] if dp[amount] != INF else -1
```

Model answer 
```js
export function countOdds(low, high) {
  if (low > high) return 0;
  const count = Math.floor((high - low) / 2);
  return low % 2 !== 0 ? count + 1 : count;
}
```

task-0247-min-initial-health-in-grid · Min initial health in grid

Prompt 
Write `calculate_min_hp(dungeon)` in Python. `dungeon` is an m x n grid; negative cells reduce health, positive cells restore it, 0 is neutral. You start at top-left and move only right/down; health can never drop to 0 or below. Return the minimum initial health needed to reach bottom-right. DP from bottom-right.

Output ONLY the function definition (e.g. `def ...`). No markdown fences, no explanation, no main block.
Model answer 
```python
def calculate_min_hp(dungeon):
    if not dungeon or not dungeon[0]:
        return 1
    m = len(dungeon)
    n = len(dungeon[0])
    dp = [[0] * n for _ in range(m)]

    dp[-1][-1] = max(1, 1 - dungeon[-1][-1])

    for j in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
        dp[-1][j] = max(1, dp[-1][j + 1] - dungeon[-1][j])

    for i in range(m - 2, -1, -1):
        dp[i][-1] = max(1, dp[i + 1][-1] - dungeon[i][-1])

    for i in range(m - 2, -1, -1):
        for j in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            min_needed = min(dp[i + 1][j], dp[i][j + 1])
            dp[i][j] = max(1, min_needed - dungeon[i][j])

    return dp[0][0]
```

task-0203-first-missing-positive · First missing positive

Prompt 
Return the smallest missing positive integer from nums (treat as unsorted; O(n) time, O(1) space). Example: [3,4,-1,1] -> 2, [1,2,0] -> 3.

```js
// BUGGY
export function firstMissingPositive(nums) {
  const present = new Set(nums);
  let x = 1;
  while (present.has(x)) x++;
  return x;
}
```

Rewrite the function so it is correct. Output ONLY the corrected function as an ES module exporting `firstMissingPositive`. No markdown fences, no explanation. (Note: a Set-based solution does not satisfy the O(1) space requirement, so implement the in-place index-marking or constant-space approach.)
Model answer 
export function firstMissingPositive(nums) {
  const n = nums.length;
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    while (nums[i] >= 1 && nums[i] <= n && nums[nums[i] - 1] !== nums[i]) {
      const target = nums[i];
      const tmp = nums[target - 1];
      nums[target - 1] = nums[i];
      nums[i] = tmp;
    }
  }
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    if (nums[i] !== i + 1) {
      return i + 1;
    }
  }
  return n + 1;
}

task-0281-election-scrutineer · Election scrutineer

Prompt 
An election-counting agent must find the candidate who received strictly more than half of the votes. Given `nums` where each value is a vote for candidate `nums[i]`, a majority candidate is guaranteed to exist. Write `majorityElement(nums)` returning the majority value. Use Boyer-Moore voting for O(n) time and O(1) space.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting the function (e.g. `export function f(...)`). Do not include markdown fences, explanation, wrapper script, or tests.
Model answer 
export function majorityElement(nums) {
  let candidate = null;
  let count = 0;
  for (const num of nums) {
    if (count === 0) {
      candidate = num;
    }
    count += (num === candidate) ? 1 : -1;
  }
  return candidate;
}

task-0092-greatest-common-divisor-of-strings · Greatest common divisor of strings

Prompt

Output ONLY the ES module exporting `gcdOfStrings`.
Model answer 
export function gcdOfStrings(str1, str2) {
  const gcd = (a, b) => (b === 0 ? a : gcd(b, a % b));
  if (str1 + str2 !== str2 + str1) return "";
  return str1.slice(0, gcd(str1.length, str2.length));
}

task-0147-merge-k-sorted-lists · Merge k sorted lists

Prompt 
Write `mergeKLists(lists)` where lists is an array of sorted (ascending) integer arrays. Merge them all into one sorted array and return it.

Output ONLY the function definition as an ES module exporting `mergeKLists`. No markdown fences, no explanation, no tests.
Model answer 
```js
export function mergeKLists(lists) {
  // Min-heap based k-way merge
  class MinHeap {
    constructor() {
      this.heap = [];
    }

    size() {
      return this.heap.length;
    }

    _left(i) { return 2 * i + 1; }
    _right(i) { return 2 * i + 2; }
    _parent(i) { return Math.floor((i - 1) / 2); }

    _swap(a, b) {
      const tmp = this.heap[a];
      this.heap[a] = this.heap[b];
      this.heap[b] = tmp;
    }

    // Compare by value, then by list index for stable tie-breaking
    _less(a, b) {
      const va = this.heap[a].value;
      const vb = this.heap[b].value;
      if (va < vb) return true;
      if (va > vb) return false;
      return this.heap[a].index < this.heap[b].index;
    }

    _bubbleUp(i) {
      while (i > 0 && this._less(i, this._parent(i))) {
        this._swap(i, this._parent(i));
        i = this._parent(i);
      }
    }

    _sinkDown(i) {
      const n = this.heap.length;
      while (true) {
        let smallest = i;
        const l = this._left(i);
        const r = this._right(i);
        if (l < n && this._less(l, smallest)) smallest = l;
        if (r < n && this._less(r, smallest)) smallest = r;
        if (smallest === i) break;
        this._swap(i, smallest);
        i = smallest;
      }
    }

    push(item) {
      this.heap.push(item);
      this._bubbleUp(this.heap.length - 1);
    }

    pop() {
      const top = this.heap[0];
      const last = this.heap.pop();
      if (this.heap.length > 0) {
        this.heap[0] = last;
        this._sinkDown(0);
      }
      return top;
    }
  }

  const heap = new MinHeap();
  const result = [];

  for (let i = 0; i < lists.length; i++) {
    if (lists[i] && lists[i].length > 0) {
      heap.push({ value: lists[i][0], index: i, pos: 0 });
    }
  }

  while (heap.size() > 0) {
    const node = heap.pop();
    result.push(node.value);
    const list = lists[node.index];
    if (node.pos + 1 < list.length) {
      heap.push({ value: list[node.pos + 1], index: node.index, pos: node.pos + 1 });
    }
  }

  return result;
}
```
Coding · Bugfix Level 2 PASS 7/7 cases
100%
